# TakeTray: Code Flow

How execution moves between files and functions in the TakeTray app. Updated by every task that adds or changes a flow, in the same commit as the code.

**Rules for this file**
- One section per flow (app start, scan for new clips, tag a clip, send to VN…).
- Write each flow as numbered steps: `file › function` and what it does or hands on, in call order. Mark the Dart ↔ Kotlin boundary with **[bridge]**.
- Paths are relative to `app/` unless they start with `packages/` or `tool/`.
- When code moves or is renamed, update the flow in the same commit. A flow that no longer matches the code is a bug.

---

## Flows

### App start

1. `main.dart › main`: calls `runAppWithLogging(body, logDir: getApplicationSupportDirectory)` and nothing else; `ensureInitialized` happens inside the guarded zone (see "Error logging").
2. `core/logging/logging_setup.dart › runAppWithLogging`: runs `body` in that zone, which calls `runApp(const ProviderScope(child: TakeTrayApp()))`.
3. `app.dart › TakeTrayApp` (a `ConsumerStatefulWidget`): `initState` calls `ref.read(backgroundSyncProvider).start()` once, which writes the phone's copy of the background state now and after every change (D-106, "Background bridge"). `build`: `ref.watch(appRouterProvider)` and builds `MaterialApp.router` with `builder: (context, child) => LaunchActionsListener(child: child!)` (the notification taps, D-107, "Notification taps") and `scaffoldMessengerKey: ref.watch(rootMessengerKeyProvider)` (the root messenger that outlives screens, D-85), `AppLocalizations` delegates, locales, `onGenerateTitle` and the TakeTray themes: `theme: TakeTrayTheme.light()`, `darkTheme: TakeTrayTheme.dark()`; `themeMode` is left at its default, `ThemeMode.system`, so the app follows the phone's light/dark setting (D-50).
4. `router/app_router.dart › appRouter` (`appRouterProvider`, kept alive): creates the `GoRouter` with `initialLocation: Routes.reels` (the platform's initial route is always `/`, so this applies: `MainActivity.getInitialRoute` returns null and Flutter's deep linking is off, D-103), the `onException` handler and the `StatefulShellRoute.indexedStack` with branches reels, toTag, settings; disposes the router with the provider.
5. `router/app_router.dart › onException`: called by go_router for any location no route matches (unknown top-level paths such as `/nope` and unknown paths below a tab root such as `/reels/stale` or `/to-tag/a/b`); logs `Logger('router').warning` without the location and calls `router.go(Routes.reels)`, so go_router's error page never shows.
6. `features/shell/views/app_shell.dart › AppShell.build`: builds a `Scaffold(extendBody: true)` whose body is the active branch (`navigationShell`) and whose `bottomNavigationBar` is the floating `TtNavPill` (items Reels, To tag, Settings with the `navReels`/`navToTag`/`navSettings` labels; the To tag badge count is a constant 0 until phase 5, and its semantics label is `toTagBadgeSemantics`). The body is wrapped in a `MediaQuery` that gives back the device's plain bottom inset, because `extendBody` otherwise adds the pill's height to it and `TtNavPill.reservedHeight` would count the pill twice (D-50). The Reels branch shows `ReelsPlaceholderScreen` (a large `TtAppBar` and `ShootingModeSection`, see "To tag, the Shooting mode card and Add earlier videos"), the To tag branch `ToTagScreen` (D-76), and the Settings branch its placeholder screen (`features/settings/views/settings_placeholder_screen.dart`: a `Scaffold` with a large `TtAppBar` titled with the localized tab name).
7. `app/media_lifecycle.dart › MediaLifecycle` wraps the `MaterialApp.router` that step 3 builds, and starts the media work on the first frame (see "App start and resume").

### Tab navigation

1. `packages/taketray_ui/lib/src/components/nav_pill.dart › TtNavPill` (an item's `InkWell.onTap`, selected or not) calls `onSelected(i)`; `features/shell/views/app_shell.dart` passes `(target) =>` which calls `navigationShell.goBranch(target, initialLocation: target == currentIndex)`. Each branch keeps its own navigator, so a tab returns to where it was; tapping the active tab again resets it to its root.
2. Android back on a tab other than Reels: `GoRouterDelegate.popRoute` first offers the pop to the branch navigator (a pushed route in that tab closes first); if that has nothing to pop, the root navigator reaches `AppShell`'s `PopScope` (`canPop: currentIndex == 0`).
3. `AppShell › PopScope.onPopInvokedWithResult`: when `didPop` is false (index is not 0) it calls `navigationShell.goBranch(0)`, so the app stays open on Reels. On Reels, `canPop` is true and the system closes the app as usual.
4. Code that calls `router.go('/to-tag')` etc. lands on the same branch switch; a location no route matches goes through `onException` in "App start" step 5.

### Android back with a menu open

1. `packages/taketray_ui/lib/src/components/menu.dart › _TtMenuButtonState`: `MenuAnchor.onOpen` / `onClose` keep an `_open` flag, which is the `canPop` of a `PopScope` around the anchor (D-51).
2. System back → `GoRouterDelegate.popRoute` (as in "Tab navigation" step 2) offers the pop to the innermost navigator; the route holding the open menu has a `PopScope` with `canPop: false`, so the page does not pop and `onPopInvokedWithResult(didPop: false)` calls `MenuController.close`. The menu closes and the screen stays. With the menu closed `canPop` is true again, so the next back pops the page (or reaches `AppShell`'s `PopScope`) as before.

### System bar icons

1. `packages/taketray_ui/lib/src/components/app_bar.dart › TtAppBar.build`: wraps the bar in `AnnotatedRegion<SystemUiOverlayStyle>` with `ttSystemUiStyle(background)` for the bar's background (`surface`, or `secondaryContainer` in selection mode).
2. `components/video_surface.dart › TtVideoSurface.build`: the same, for the black `videoBackground`.
3. `components/internal/system_ui.dart › ttSystemUiStyle`: `ThemeData.estimateBrightnessForColor(background)`; a light background gives `SystemUiOverlayStyle.dark` (dark icons), a dark one `.light`. It sets `systemNavigationBarIconBrightness` the same way, because the constants assume a black navigation bar, and makes the status bar, navigation bar and divider transparent with `background.withValues(alpha: 0)`.
4. Each frame, Flutter's `RenderView._updateSystemChrome` reads the style at the middle of the status bar (and, on Android, of the navigation bar); a lone region at the top supplies both. The first region found under a screen's `TtAppBar` or `TtVideoSurface` therefore sets the icon colours for that screen (D-51).

### Database open and migrations

Repositories read `appDatabaseProvider`, and the first screen that uses one opens it. See D-54 and, for schema v2, D-120.

1. `data/database/connection.dart › appDatabase` (`appDatabaseProvider`, kept alive): `AppDatabase(driftDatabase(name: 'taketray'))`, which is `taketray.sqlite` in the app's private documents folder (drift_flutter, SQLite from the `sqlite3` package's build hooks); `ref.onDispose(db.close)`. Tests call `test/support/test_database.dart › testDatabase` instead, which passes `NativeDatabase.memory()`.
2. Drift opens the file lazily, on the first query, and reads `PRAGMA user_version` (0 for a new file).
3. `data/database/app_database.dart › AppDatabase.migration`:
   1. `user_version` 0 (new file): `onCreate` runs `m.createAll()`, which creates the ten tables of `data/database/tables.dart` (with the `clips` identity `CHECK`s; the Dart classes `EditSessions` and `EditCopies` are the SQL tables `vn_sessions` and `vn_copies`, D-120) and the indexes `clips_date_taken_utc`, `clip_reels_reel_id`, `vn_copies_clip_id` and `sent_clips_clip_id`.
   2. `user_version` equal to `schemaVersion` (2): nothing to migrate.
   3. `user_version` above `schemaVersion` (a database from a newer TakeTray): `onUpgrade` throws `UnsupportedError` before anything else. Drift writes the new `user_version` only after `onUpgrade` succeeds, so the file is left as it was and the query fails.
   4. `user_version` 1: `onUpgrade` opens one `transaction` and runs `m.runMigrationSteps(from: 1, to: 2, steps: migrationSteps(from1To2: AppDatabase._from1To2))` (`migrationSteps` is generated in `data/database/app_database.steps.dart`). `_from1To2`: `ALTER TABLE vn_sessions ADD COLUMN editor` (default `'vn'`: every v1 session was VN), `ALTER TABLE vn_copies ADD COLUMN copied_at` (default 0), `UPDATE vn_copies SET copied_at = COALESCE((SELECT created_at FROM vn_sessions s WHERE s.reel_id = vn_copies.reel_id), 0)` (each copy takes its session's start; a copy with no session keeps 0 rather than failing the upgrade), `CREATE TABLE sent_clips` and `CREATE INDEX sent_clips_clip_id`. Drift's `runMigrationSteps` then writes `user_version = 2` on the same connection, inside the transaction, and the transaction commits. Any failure rolls the whole step back: the file is exactly v1 again and the next open tries once more.
   5. `beforeOpen`: `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON`, on every open, so `ON DELETE CASCADE` works (`vn_copies.clip_id` and `sent_clips.clip_id` have no foreign key on purpose, D-54, D-120).
4. Drift sets `user_version` to `schemaVersion` and runs the waiting query. Instants are read and written through `data/database/converters.dart › UtcMillisConverter` (UTC milliseconds in int columns), enums through `textEnum` (by name).

Changing the schema (by hand, `cd app`):
1. Edit `data/database/tables.dart` and bump `AppDatabase.schemaVersion`.
2. `../tool/dart run build_runner build`, then `../tool/dart run drift_dev make-migrations`: it dumps the new version to `drift_schemas/taketray/drift_schema_v<N>.json`, rewrites `lib/data/database/app_database.steps.dart` (the `migrationSteps` / `stepByStep` helpers, now with `from<N-1>To<N>`) and the schema helpers in `test/drift/taketray/generated/`. It writes `test/drift/taketray/migration_test.dart` only when there is none, so from v3 on add the new data test to the existing file by hand.
3. Pass the new step to `migrationSteps(from1To2: …, from2To3: …)` in `onUpgrade` (keep the `from > to` guard and the one transaction) and write it; fill in the data-integrity test with real-looking rows of the old version.
4. `../tool/flutter test test/drift`: `migration_test.dart` migrates every older dump to every newer one and checks the schema, checks the v1 rows come through (D-120), that a failed step leaves the old file as it was and that a phone killed before Drift's version write opens normally; `schema_test.dart` opens every dump with today's `AppDatabase` and compares the result with the tables in code.
5. Commit the code, the dump and the generated files together; `tool/check_generated` (step 4 of "Local checks") fails while any of them is stale, the steps file and the schema helpers included. Editing a table without bumping the version fails there too: `make-migrations` exits with "A schema for version 2 of the taketray database already exists and differs from the current schema", and `schema_test.dart` reports the differing column.

### Deleting a reel

The reel screens (phase 4, Task 7) call this. See D-56 and D-84.

1. Phase 8, before step 2: the caller deletes the reel's copy files from `Movies/TakeTray/` (the `vn_copies` rows say which) and ends its editing session (R8, D-120). Once the reel is deleted, nothing records them.
2. `data/repositories/reels_repository.dart › ReelsRepository.delete(id)`: opens `_db.transaction`, so steps 3 to 5 all happen or none does.
3. Reads what Undo needs (D-84): the `reels` row (null for an unknown id), its `clip_reels`, `reel_references`, `vn_sessions` and `vn_copies` rows (none on the app's path, which ended the session; `sent_clips` rows are not kept).
4. Deletes the `reels` row. SQLite's `ON DELETE CASCADE` (foreign keys are on, D-54) removes its `clip_reels`, `reel_references` and `vn_sessions` rows, and the `vn_copies` and `sent_clips` rows of those sessions.
5. `data/repositories/undo_writer.dart › UndoWriter.sweepOrphans`: `AppDatabase.selectOrphanClips` reads every column of the clips that have no `clip_reels` row and no `inbox` row, then `AppDatabase.deleteOrphanClips` deletes them (`data/database/housekeeping.dart`, one predicate for both). A clip still in another reel, in To tag or in Skipped stays. `vn_copies` rows are not touched (no foreign key to `clips`, D-54). This runs even when the id was unknown, so it also removes any orphan that was already there.
6. The transaction commits; the repository logs the reel id, how many reels and how many clips were removed (never the name, D-10), and returns a `DeleteReelUndo` with all of it. Any `watchAll` / `watchById` / `watchReelSummaries` stream re-queries and emits again. Undo: see "Undo" below.

### Reel names, descriptions and summaries (`ReelsRepository`, D-84)

- `create(name, description:)` and `rename(id, name)`: the name is checked first (`checkReelName`: 1 to 40 characters after `normalizeName`), and a description over 1,000 characters (`checkReelDescription`, also in `setDescription`) throws `InvalidInputException(ReelDescriptionProblem.tooLong)`. Then, inside the write's transaction, every reel's name is read and compared in Dart with `reelNameKey` (`normalizeName` + `toLowerCase`, which folds "É" too; SQL's `lower()` folds only ASCII). A match throws `InvalidInputException(ReelNameProblem.duplicate)` and nothing is written. `rename` reads the reel first (`StateError` if it is gone) and skips the check when the new name has the same key as the current one, so "Goa" → "GOA" is fine even while an imported "goa" exists. Two saves at once run one after the other, so only one gets the name. Backup import and Undo of a delete insert rows directly and never check names.
- `watchReelSummaries()`: one `customSelect` per emission, re-run after any change to `reels`, `clip_reels` or `clips`. An `agg` CTE counts each reel's links, its missing clips and the duration of the others; a `cover` CTE numbers each reel's clips that pass `hasPicture` (`data/database/clip_rules.dart`, the rule `watchFirstShowableClip` uses too) by `date_taken_utc`, then id, and keeps the first; both are `LEFT JOIN`ed to `reels`, ordered by `created_at DESC, id DESC`. The cover's columns come back as `"cover.<column>"` and are read with `clips.map(row, tablePrefix: 'cover')`.

### Tagging data changes

The scanner and the verifier ("Finding new clips", "Checking clips still exist" below) call `addCheckedClips` and `applyVerification`; the tag player (phase 5) and the reel screens (phase 4 onwards) will call the rest. All in `data/repositories/clips_repository.dart › ClipsRepository`; every method that writes is one transaction, and every stream re-queries after any of the tables it reads change. See D-57. A clip's identity is `volumeName` + `mediaStoreId` (or `pickerUri`); "find" below means looking a clip up by that.

| Method | Writes |
|---|---|
| `addNewClipsToReel(reelId, clips)` | For each clip: find, or insert a `clips` row. Then a `clip_reels` row (`addedAt` = now) for each clip the reel did not have, and `reels.updatedAt` = now if any was added. No `inbox` row is created; the clips' existing `inbox` rows (pending or skipped) are deleted. |
| `tagClip(clipId, reelIds)` | Deletes the `clip_reels` rows of reels not in `reelIds`; inserts rows (`addedAt` = now) for reels the clip was not in; rows that stay are not touched (`addedAt` kept). Deletes the clip's `inbox` row. `reels.updatedAt` = now for every reel that gained or lost the clip. `ArgumentError` if `reelIds` is empty. |
| `untagToInbox(clipId)` | Deletes all the clip's `clip_reels` rows; writes its `inbox` row as `pending` (`addedAt` = now); `reels.updatedAt` = now for the reels that lost it. |
| `skipClips(ids)` | One transaction (D-94): reads the `inbox` rows of those ids that are `pending` (batches of 500), then sets their `state` = `skipped` (`addedAt` kept). A skipped clip, a clip in a reel (no `inbox` row) and an unknown id are left alone. Returns an `InboxUndo` (the rows it moved, as they were, by clip id; `after` = `skipped`). Nothing pending among the ids (or no ids): nothing written, an empty record. |
| `skipAllPending()` | One transaction (D-94) that **reads and writes**: every `pending` row is read, then `UPDATE inbox SET state = 'skipped' WHERE state = 'pending'`; the count written must equal the count read (else `StateError`, rolled back). A scan's insert queued meanwhile waits for the transaction, so it is either in the record or still `pending`, never skipped behind the record's back. `addedAt` kept. Returns an `InboxUndo` (`after` = `skipped`); an empty To tag writes nothing. No screen calls it now: Skip all skips the ids its dialog counted with `skipClips` (docs/later.md). |
| `restoreClips(ids)` | As `skipClips`, the other way: the ids that are `skipped` become `pending` (`addedAt` kept). Returns an `InboxUndo` with `after` = `pending`. |
| `removeMissingFromInbox(clipId)` | One transaction (D-116): reads the `inbox` row of that clip **only if the clip is flagged `missing`** (either state; picked and MediaStore clips alike), deletes it, then the orphan sweep (`UndoWriter.sweepOrphans`: a clip in no reel and no list is deleted, with every field in the record). Returns an `InboxRemoveUndo(removed: rows as they were, sweptClips)`; empty (nothing written) for an unknown id, a clip not flagged missing, or one with no `inbox` row. A clip that is also in a reel (not a state the app makes, D-57) keeps its link, its row in the reel and the clip; only the list row goes. No reel is touched, and neither is the video file or a `vn_copies` row (D-54). |
| `addClipsToReel(reelId, ids)` | A `clip_reels` row for each clip the reel did not have; `reels.updatedAt` = now if any was added. The clips' `inbox` rows (pending or skipped) are deleted. Returns an `AddUndo` (the links it created, the `inbox` rows it deleted, the reel's `updatedAt` before; D-84). |
| `removeClipsFromReel(reelId, ids)` | Deletes those `clip_reels` rows; `reels.updatedAt` = now if any was removed; then the orphan sweep, reading the rows first (`UndoWriter.sweepOrphans`: a clip in no reel and no list is deleted; its `vn_copies` row stays). Returns a `RemoveUndo` (the removed links with their `addedAt`, every field of the swept clips, the reel's `updatedAt` before; D-84). Its Undo also re-points the reel's `sent_clips` rows of a swept clip that came back under another id (D-120). |
| `addClipsToReels(reelIds, ids)` | The same add for each reel of the set, in the set's order, in **one** transaction (D-84 point 8). Returns one `AddUndo` for each reel. A reel or clip that does not exist, or a failed write, throws and rolls everything back: no reel gains a link. Undo the list last to first. |
| `moveClips(fromReelId, toReelId, ids)` | `ArgumentError` when the two are the same, `StateError` (nothing written) when either reel is unknown. For each clip the source has: first a `clip_reels` row in the target (`addedAt` = now) if the target did not have it, then the source row is deleted and the orphan sweep runs, so no moved clip is ever without a place. `updatedAt` = now for each reel that gained or lost a link. Returns a `MoveUndo` (the target links it created, not the ones that were there; the removed source links; both reels' `updatedAt` before; D-84). |
| `undo(record)`, `undoAll(records)` | See "Undo" below. |
| `addCheckedClips(clips, sameVideo:)` | One transaction (D-72, Ruling U); the only way detected clips are saved (it replaced `addDetectedClips`, which nothing in the app called). For each clip: a stored MediaStore clip with the same volume and number that `sameVideo(stored, clip)` accepts is found (nothing written; its id is returned, it keeps its `firstSeenAt` and its place, so a rescan changes nothing). One it rejects is detached (`mediaStoreId = -clipId`, `missing`, `lastVerifiedAt` = now) and the clip is inserted as new with an `inbox` row `pending`, as is a clip whose identity nobody holds. Then the detached clips' `inbox` rows go (if flagged missing) and `deleteOrphanClips()` runs (Ruling Y); a detached clip in a reel keeps its links. Returns `DetectedClips(ids, inserted, detached)`. |
| `mediaStoreClipsHolding(volumeName, numbers)` | Nothing (a read): the attached MediaStore clips on that volume holding one of the numbers, by number. Picked and detached clips are not in it. Batches of 500. |
| `applyVerification(found:, gone:, otherVideo:, flagOnly:)` | One transaction (D-72, M8): `otherVideo` detached (`mediaStoreId = -clipId`, `missing = true`, `lastVerifiedAt` = now; unknown ids and picked clips are ignored, an already detached clip keeps its negative id); `gone` flagged missing and `found` not missing, both `lastVerifiedAt` = now; `flagOnly` clips (the picked clips whose address cannot be read, D-116) are flagged missing like `gone` and then left alone: they keep their `inbox` row, links and address, and a later `found` un-flags them; then the `inbox` rows of `gone` and `otherVideo` (only those flagged missing in the same transaction, so a picked clip or a found one keeps its row) go and `deleteOrphanClips()` runs; a clip in a reel keeps its links and its row, flagged. Empty sets (all four) write and sweep nothing. `ArgumentError`, nothing written, for a clip in two sets (`flagOnly` included). |
| `clipsByIds(ids)` / `allClipIds()` | Nothing (reads): the clips that exist, by id (batches of 500; unknown ids left out) / every clip id. |
| `mediaStoreClipsOn(volumeName)` | Nothing (a read): the MediaStore clips of that volume with `mediaStoreId > 0`, by id. Detached clips, picked clips and other volumes are left out. |
| `detachedClipsOn(volumeName)` | Nothing (a read): the detached MediaStore clips of that volume (`mediaStoreId < 0`), by id. Attached clips, picked clips and other volumes are left out. |
| `reattachDetached(volumeName, matches)` | One transaction (D-108, I-3). `matches` maps a detached clip's id to the MediaStore id of its video. For each pair, in clip id order: the clip must still be a detached MediaStore clip of that volume (else ignored). **No clip holds the number:** the clip takes it, `missing = false`, `lastVerifiedAt` = now. **A clip N holds it and is `sameFingerprint` with the detached clip D:** N's reel links are merged into D (a reel that has both keeps one link with the earlier `addedAt`; a reel that had only N gets a link for D with N's `addedAt`), a D in no reel and no list takes N's `inbox` row, N's `inbox` row, links and `clips` row are deleted, and D takes the number. **Held by a clip that is another video, or by a picked clip:** nothing changes. A reel's `updatedAt` is not touched. Returns `ReattachResult(reattached, merged, left)`. `ArgumentError`, nothing written, for two clips with one number or a number below 1; a database failure rolls everything back. |
| `applyRebuildMatch(volumeName, match)` | One transaction (D-71). First it selects the eligible clips of the match: on that volume, source `mediaStore`, `mediaStoreId > 0` (a detached clip from a stale match, a picked clip, another volume and an unknown id are ignored). Then `mediaStoreId = -clipId` for every eligible clip in `match.remap` and `match.lost`. Then, if a clip outside the match holds one of the eligible new ids, `StateError` and everything is rolled back. Then each `remap` clip gets its new `mediaStoreId`, `missing = false`, `lastVerifiedAt` = now; each `lost` clip keeps `-clipId` with `missing = true`, `lastVerifiedAt` = now. Then the `inbox` rows of the lost clips (flagged missing) go and `deleteOrphanClips()` runs (Ruling Y, D-72): a lost clip in a reel stays there; one in no reel is deleted. `ArgumentError`, nothing written, for a clip in both sets, two clips with one new id, or a new id below 1. An empty match writes nothing. |

Streams (`watchInbox(state)`, `watchPendingCount()`, `watchReelClips(reelId)`, `watchReelIdsOf(clipId)`) write nothing. The first and third sort by `clips.dateTakenUtc`, ties by `clips.id`. `watchReelClips` gives `ReelClip`s: the clip, how many reels it is in (a `COUNT` subquery over `clip_reels`, in the same statement) and the `addedAt` of its link to this reel.

A clip that is lost or detached holds the negative id `-clipId` and keeps its fingerprint. **Every path that loses or detaches a clip also takes it out of To tag and Skipped, in the same transaction** (Ruling Y, D-72): `applyRebuildMatch`, `addCheckedClips` and `applyVerification` delete the `inbox` rows of those clips that are flagged missing and run `deleteOrphanClips()`. A clip in a reel keeps its links and its row, flagged missing; a clip in no reel is deleted. That number is never a real MediaStore id (those are positive) and is unique to the clip (a clip id is the primary key), and a backup import refuses any other negative number, so the video that later gets the old number is a new clip (`addCheckedClips` does not find the old row, D-71). **The way back (D-108):** for a day after a rebuild that detached clips (its re-match window), a scan of the stable volume matches those clips against the videos now on it whenever the volume's generation has moved (`reattachDetached`, "Finding new clips"), so a clip that was lost only because Android had not indexed its video yet gets it back, and a duplicate that came in as a new clip meanwhile gives way to it. A clip a verify detached has no way back.

Unknown ids: an unknown reel throws `StateError` in `addNewClipsToReel`, `tagClip`, `addClipsToReel` and `removeClipsFromReel`; an unknown clip throws `StateError` in `tagClip`, `untagToInbox` and `addClipsToReel`. Each check runs before the first write, so nothing is changed. `skipClips`, `restoreClips`, `removeClipsFromReel` and `applyVerification` ignore clip ids that do not exist (`skipClips`/`restoreClips` also ignore clips with no `inbox` row, or one in the other state).

### Undo (`lib/data/repositories/undo_writer.dart`, D-84)

Every remove, move, add-to-reel, reel delete, skip, skip all and restore returns an `UndoRecord` (`lib/domain/models/undo_record.dart`): `RemoveUndo`, `MoveUndo`, `AddUndo`, `DeleteReelUndo`, `InboxUndo` (D-94) or `InboxRemoveUndo` (D-116, Remove on a missing tile in To tag and Skipped). The tag player tags with `addClipsToReels` (one `AddUndo` per reel) and skips with `skipClips` (an `InboxUndo`); To tag uses `skipClips` (Skip on a selection, and Skip all with the ids its dialog counted) and Skipped uses `restoreClips`; `skipAllPending` has no caller now (docs/later.md). Each is a snapshot taken inside the action's own transaction, holding plain values and domain models only. The snack (Task 7 and 9) keeps it until it closes and passes it to `ReelsRepository.undoAll` (a list of one or more; `ClipsRepository` has the same `undo` / `undoAll`, all on `UndoWriter`). Every time a repository writes into a record is the stored value: repositories take the clock's time from `Clock.storedNow()` (milliseconds, as `UtcMillisConverter` stores), so a comparison in Dart agrees with the database (D-84 point 9).

1. `UndoWriter.undoAll(records)` (and `undo(record)`, which is `undoAll([record])`) opens **one** `_db.transaction` for the whole list and goes through it **last to first**. A record object undone already (an `Expando` mark, set when its steps are done) is skipped with a log line: a second undo, or a double tap queued behind the first, changes nothing. If any step of any record fails, the transaction rolls back, every mark this call set is cleared, and the same list can be undone again: nothing of it stays undone (D-84 point 9).
2. **A reel that is gone** (deleted meanwhile): `RemoveUndo` and `AddUndo` restore nothing and log a warning with the id. `MoveUndo` restores what it can (see 6). `DeleteReelUndo` whose reel id exists already (undone, or the id given away by a backup import) restores nothing. Nothing throws for it.
3. **Swept clips** (`RemoveUndo`, `DeleteReelUndo`): for each swept clip that a restored link needs, in this order:
   1. a row with the clip's old id that is the same clip (same source, size, duration, date taken and `firstSeenAt`) is used as it is (undone already, or restored by a backup);
   2. a clip that holds its identity now (the same volume and MediaStore number above 0, or the same picker URI: the table's unique keys) **and** has the same fingerprint (`sameFingerprint`: size above 0 and equal, duration, `dateTakenUtc` instant; name ignored) is linked instead and nothing is inserted: the video came back through Add earlier videos, and there is never a second row for it;
   3. a different clip holding the old id (only a backup import can do that) means this clip is skipped, with a warning, rather than linking the wrong video;
   4. no holder: the clip is inserted with its old id and every field (`InsertMode.insertOrAbort`). A detached clip (number `-id`) has no identity to find, so it comes back as itself;
   5. a holder with another fingerprint (a rebuild gave its number to another video): a MediaStore clip is inserted with its old id but detached and missing (`mediaStoreId = -id`, `missing`, `lastVerifiedAt` = now, D-71), so it shows the missing tile; a picked clip is skipped with a warning.
4. **Links** come back with their old `addedAt` when the reel and the clip exist and the link is not already there; each restored link deletes that clip's `inbox` row (a clip is never in a reel and a list at once, D-57).
5. **`updatedAt`** goes back to its value before the action only while the reel still holds the value the action wrote, so a rename after the remove is kept.
6. **Per record:**
   - `RemoveUndo`: clips (3), links (4), `updatedAt` (5).
   - `MoveUndo`: first the source links (4), then each created target link is deleted if it still has the `addedAt` the move gave it and the clip has another place (a link or a list); a clip with no other place keeps it (its source reel may be gone), so undoing a move never deletes a clip. Then both `updatedAt`s (5). No sweep.
   - `AddUndo`: first puts back each deleted `inbox` row with its state and `addedAt` if the clip still exists, is in no list, and has no link except the ones this add created; then deletes each created link (same `addedAt` only) whose clip keeps another place, as for a move, so undoing an add never deletes a clip. No sweep. Then `updatedAt` (5).
   - `InboxRemoveUndo` (D-116): each swept clip a removed row needs comes back as in (3), with its old id (or the id of the clip that holds its identity now); then each row is inserted with its state and `addedAt` **only while that clip exists, has no `inbox` row and no reel link** (a clip put in a reel since stays there; one already in To tag or Skipped keeps that row). A swept clip no row needs stays gone. Idempotent through the once-only mark.
   - `InboxUndo` (D-94): for each row in `before`, `UPDATE inbox SET state = before.state WHERE clip_id = … AND state = after AND added_at = before.addedAt`: a row goes back only while it is exactly as the skip or restore left it (the action moved only the state). A clip tagged since has no row (it stays in its reel, and never gets a second place); one restored since (for a skip record), skipped since (for a restore record) or put back in To tag since (another state, or a new `addedAt`) keeps what happened to it; a clip a scan added after a skip all is not in the record. A stale record still undoes a row that went back and forth to exactly its `after` state; accepted, because a new Undo snack replaces the previous one, so only the latest action offers Undo. No reel, link or clip is touched. The log line holds the state and counts.
   - `DeleteReelUndo`: inserts the reel row with its old id and every field (no duplicate-name check: restoring wins, D-80), then clips (3), links (4), references with their ids (a new id only if a backup gave the old one away), the VN session and its copy rows. Every copy row comes back (D-54: the row is the only record of a copy file and may outlive its clip); a swept clip that came back under another id gives the row that id, and one that could not come back leaves the row with its old id, which no clip will hold (ids are never reused).
7. The transaction commits; the log line holds the record type, reel ids and counts only (D-10). The streams that read those tables emit again.

### References, VN folders, settings and time zones

Nothing calls these yet; the reference screens (phase 4 onwards), the editor handoff (phase 8), settings (phase 10) and the native zone writer (phase 3) will. See D-58. Each is its own class in `data/repositories/`, built from `(AppDatabase, Clock)`.

**Adding a reference** (`references_repository.dart › ReferencesRepository.add(reelId, rawUrl, {label})`):

1. `normalizeReferenceUrl(rawUrl)` cleans the link (D-55). `null` throws `InvalidInputException(ReferenceUrlProblem.invalid)`.
2. `checkReferenceLabel(label)`; a label over 60 characters throws `InvalidInputException(ReferenceLabelProblem.tooLong)`. Both throws happen before the database is touched.
3. `_db.transaction`: an unknown reel throws `StateError`. Then it looks for a `reel_references` row with the same `reelId` and cleaned `url`.
4. Found: returns `ReferenceAlreadySaved(existing)`; nothing is written and the new label is ignored.
5. Not found: inserts the row (`source` from `referenceSourceOf`, `label` trimmed and null when blank, `createdAt` = now), sets `reels.updatedAt` = now in the same transaction, and returns `ReferenceAdded(reference)`. `ReferenceAlreadySaved` does not touch the reel.

`setLabel(id, label)` checks the label, then in a transaction updates the row and sets its reel's `updatedAt` = now (unknown id: `StateError`, nothing changes). `delete(id)` deletes one row and sets its reel's `updatedAt` = now in the same transaction (unknown id: nothing changes). `watchForReel` is newest first; `watchCounts` is reel id to count, without the reels that have none.

**Editing session** (`edit_sessions_repository.dart › EditSessionsRepository`, D-120; SQL tables `vn_sessions`, `vn_copies`, `sent_clips`):

| Method | Writes |
|---|---|
| `startSession(reelId, editor, base, {alsoTaken})` | Trims `base` (blank: `ArgumentError`). One transaction: unknown reel or a reel that already has a session throws `StateError`; the folder is `uniqueFolderName(base, {every vn_sessions.folderName} + alsoTaken)`; inserts the `vn_sessions` row with `editor` and `created_at` = now. |
| `recordSent(reelId, clipIds)` | One transaction. No session: `StateError`, nothing written (also for an empty list). Reads which ids are already in `sent_clips`, inserts the others with `sent_at` = now (a clip sent before keeps its first time) and returns the `Set<int>` it inserted, so a failed share can take back exactly that. |
| `removeSent(reelId, clipIds)` | Deletes the `sent_clips` rows of that reel with those ids. Unknown ids, an empty list, an unknown reel or session and other reels' rows are untouched. |
| `recordCopies(copies)` | One transaction, all or none. Every copy's reel needs a session (`StateError`). Per copy: a row with the same reel and clip and the same `copy_media_store_id` is skipped; with another copy id, or with a `copy_media_store_id` the reel already holds for another clip (stored, or earlier in the batch), it throws `StateError` and the whole batch rolls back; otherwise inserts the `vn_copies` row with its `copied_at`. An empty list does nothing. |
| `removeCopies(reelId, copyIds)` | Deletes the `vn_copies` rows of that reel with those copy ids. Unknown ids and other reels' rows are untouched; `sent_clips` stays. |
| `endSession(reelId)` | Deletes the `vn_sessions` row; `ON DELETE CASCADE` deletes its `vn_copies` and `sent_clips` rows. The caller has deleted the files first. |

`sessionOf` and `copiesOf` only read. `watchEditState(reelId)` re-reads the session, its copies and its sent ids in one transaction whenever `vn_sessions`, `vn_copies` or `sent_clips` changes and emits only when the state differs (null without a session). `watchEditors()` is reel id to editor. `watchSummaries()` is one statement: `vn_sessions` joined to `reels`, `vn_copies` left-joined and grouped (count, `COALESCE(SUM(size_bytes), 0)`, `MAX(copied_at)`), `ORDER BY created_at DESC, reel_id DESC`, re-run when sessions, copies or reels change. Logs: ids, counts and the editor's name only.

**Settings** (`settings_repository.dart › SettingsRepository`): `load()` reads the rows whose key is in `exportedKeys` and decodes each value from JSON; a missing key, bad JSON, a wrong type or minutes outside 0 to 1439 gives that setting's default, and a corrupt one logs the key name. `save(settings)` upserts the three rows in one transaction and leaves other keys alone. `watch()` re-queries when the `settings` table changes and emits only when the result differs from the last one. Only `exportedKeys` (the three keys) is ever exported by a backup; the phone-state keys below are not.

**Zone timeline** (`zone_repository.dart › ZoneRepository.record(at, zoneId)`): in a transaction, reads the last `zone_timeline` row at or before `at`; if its `zoneId` equals the new one nothing is written. Otherwise: if the last row strictly before `at` already has the new zone, the row at exactly `at` is deleted (it was the only way to get here); else a row `(at, zoneId)` is inserted (replacing a row at exactly `at`). Then the first row after `at` is deleted when it has the new zone, so the timeline never has two neighbours with the same zone. `zoneAt(instant)` reads the whole timeline, oldest first, and runs the domain `zoneAt` on it.

**Merging many entries** (`ZoneRepository.recordAll(entries)`, D-71): for entries that may arrive in any order, such as the native timeline. In one transaction it reads the whole timeline, adds the entries (cut to milliseconds; an entry at an existing instant replaces it; for two new ones at one instant the later in iteration order wins), sorts by time, drops every entry whose zone equals the one before it, and rewrites the table, only if the result differs from what is stored. An empty list does nothing. `record` is for one entry at a time and is exact only for entries that arrive in time order (D-58).

**Phone state** (`phone_state_repository.dart › PhoneStateRepository`, D-71, D-72): Shooting mode and where each volume stood live in the `settings` table under `shootingMode` (one JSON value: `on`, `since`, `baselines` by volume name; an older value may still hold `earlierAdded`, which is ignored, D-90), `mediaAccessWasFull` (a JSON boolean), `mediaVolumeVersions` (a JSON object, volume name to the MediaStore version the stored clips' numbers belong to), `mediaRematchWindows` (a JSON object, volume name to its re-match window, D-108) and `folderTipSeen` (a JSON boolean, D-114). No phone-state key is in `exportedKeys`, so a backup never holds them, and an import deletes them: Shooting mode is off afterwards. A missing row is `ShootingState.off`; a value that is not JSON, has the wrong shape, or says `on` with no `since` is off too, and only the key name is logged.

| Method | Writes |
|---|---|
| `loadShooting()` / `watchShooting()` | Nothing. The watch emits only when the state changes. |
| `turnShootingOn(baselines)` | One transaction. Shooting mode off: writes `on`, `since` = now and the baselines, and returns it. Already on: writes nothing and returns the current state (the first `since` and the baselines stay). A stored value that still has the old `earlierAdded` key (before D-90) reads the same; the next write drops it. |
| `turnShootingOff()` | Deletes the `shootingMode` row (baselines and `since` go with it). |
| `advanceBaseline(volume, expectedSession:, expected:, next:, rebuilt: false)` | One transaction, compare-and-set. Writes `next` as `volume`'s baseline, keeping the other volumes, only if all hold: Shooting mode is on with the same `since` as `expectedSession`; the stored baseline of `volume` equals `expected` (null: none stored); and, with `expected` and `next` on the same version and `rebuilt` false, `next.generation >= expected.generation`. The scanner passes `rebuilt: true` after a resync (D-72). Returns whether it wrote. |
| `volumeVersions()` / `recordVolumeVersion(volume, version)` | Read / write `mediaVolumeVersions` (one transaction; other volumes kept; unchanged → no write). Shooting mode going off does not clear it; unreadable reads as empty, logging only the key. |
| `markAccessWasFull()` / `accessWasFull()` | Sets / reads `mediaAccessWasFull`. Shooting mode going off does not clear it; unreadable reads as false. |
| `markFolderTipSeen()` / `folderTipSeen()` | Sets / reads `folderTipSeen` (Add videos' tip was dismissed with **Got it**, D-114). Shooting mode going off does not clear it; unreadable reads as false (logged by key name only). |
| `rematchWindow(volume)` / `openRematchWindow(volume, clipIds:, generation:, at:)` / `recordRematch(volume, generation)` / `closeRematchWindow(volume)` | Read / write `mediaRematchWindows` (D-108; each write one transaction, other volumes kept). Opening while a window is open at `at` keeps its clips too and restarts its 24 hours; an ended one is replaced. `recordRematch` changes only the generation, and nothing without a window. Closing the last one deletes the row. Shooting mode going off does not clear it; unreadable (any entry of the wrong shape, a time `DateTime` cannot hold) reads as no window for every volume, logging only the key; `rematchWindow` then deletes the value, so it is logged once. |

### Backup export and import

Nothing calls this yet. The Settings screen (phase 10) will choose the file with the Storage Access Framework and show the warning before an import; the "X of Y clips found" report after an import needs the media bridge (phase 3) and that screen (phase 10). See D-59. All in `data/backup/`.

**Export** (`backup_service.dart › BackupService.exportJson()`):

1. `_db.transaction` (one consistent snapshot) reads `reels`, `reel_references` and `clips` by id, `clip_reels` by clip then reel, `inbox` by clip, `zone_timeline` by time, and the settings through `SettingsRepository.load()` (only `exportedKeys`; a missing or corrupt one is its default). `vn_sessions`, `vn_copies`, `sent_clips` and every other `settings` key are not read.
2. `backup_format.dart › encodeBackup(data, clock.now())` writes the v1 object (`app`, `schemaVersion`, `exportedAt`, then the seven sections) with `JsonEncoder.withIndent('  ')`; times are UTC ISO-8601 with milliseconds and `Z` (`DateTime.toIso8601String`, so a year outside 0000 to 9999 is `-0001` or `+010001`).
3. Logs the row counts (never names, links, labels or file names, D-10) and returns the text.

**Import** (`backup_service.dart › BackupService.importJson(json)`):

1. `backup_format.dart › decodeBackup(json)` reads the whole file before the database is touched:
   1. `jsonDecode`; a `FormatException` → `BackupException(notJson)`.
   2. Not an object, or `app` is not "TakeTray" → `notTakeTray`. `schemaVersion` missing, not a whole number or below 1 → `notTakeTray`; above `backupSchemaVersion` (1) → `newerVersion`, without reading the rest.
   3. `_readV1`: `exportedAt`, then `reels`, `references`, `clips`, `clipReels`, `inbox`, `zoneTimeline` and `settings`, each through `_Fields`, whose getters throw `_Invalid('<path>')` for a missing key or a wrong type; the section loops check ids (1 to 2^53, unique), links to `reels` and `clips` read earlier, duplicates, names, labels, links, the clip identity rule (a detected clip has a volume and a number and no picker link, a picked clip a picker link and neither a volume nor a number, D-72; a negative `mediaStoreId` must be minus the clip's own `id`, D-71), minutes and times (D-59 lists them). Any `_Invalid` → `BackupException(invalidData, where: path)`. Text is trimmed, links cleaned as the repositories do, and times cut to whole milliseconds (as stored), so a duplicate zone instant is caught here. A time is read by `_parseInstant`: the `_instant` pattern (year `dddd`, `-dddd` or `±dddddd`), then `DateTime.utc` of the fields must give back the same fields (no 30 February, no wrapped year) and `DateTime.parse` must not throw (a time past what a `DateTime` holds is invalid, not a crash).
   4. Normalising: of the references of one reel with the same cleaned link, all but the one with the lowest id are dropped; an `inbox` row whose clip has a `clip_reels` row is dropped (the link wins, D-57); then every clip with neither a link nor an `inbox` row is dropped (the orphan rule, D-56). `BackupData.referencesDropped`, `inboxRowsDropped` and `orphanClipsDropped` count them.
   5. On any `BackupException`: logs the problem and path (no values) and rethrows. Nothing was written.
2. `_replaceAll` › `_db.transaction`: deletes every row of all ten tables, itself (not through the cascades): `sent_clips`, `vn_copies`, `vn_sessions`, `clip_reels`, `inbox`, `reel_references`, `clips`, `reels`, `settings` and `zone_timeline` (children first), then one `batch` inserts `reels`, `clips`, `clip_reels`, `inbox`, `reel_references`, the three settings rows (each value `jsonEncode`d, as `SettingsRepository.save` writes them) and `zone_timeline`, all with the file's ids. Any failure rolls the whole transaction back. A `SqliteException` with result code `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT` (directly, or as the `remoteCause` of drift's `DriftRemoteException` when the database runs on its background isolate) is logged by its code only and rethrown as `BackupException(invalidData)`; any other error is rethrown as it is.
3. Imported ids may be ids used before the import; that is safe because `vn_copies` and `sent_clips`, the only tables where a clip id outlives its clip, were emptied in the same transaction (D-54, D-120). SQLite moves each `AUTOINCREMENT` counter (`sqlite_sequence`) up to the highest inserted id and never down, so rows added later get ids above every imported one.
4. Logs the counts (and the dropped inbox rows, orphan clips and duplicate references) and returns `ImportSummary(reels, clips, references, inboxPending, inboxSkipped)`. Every `watch…` stream re-queries. Shooting mode is off afterwards (its key is gone); phase 3 resets the baselines.

### Local checks (`tool/check`)

Run from anywhere; the script changes to the repo root and stops at the first failing step. A fresh clone, or any ARB edit, needs `tool/flutter pub get` (or `tool/flutter gen-l10n`) before analyze or IDE use, because `app/lib/l10n/gen/` is generated, not committed (D-27); `tool/check` does the `pub get` itself.

`tool/check` declares `FLUTTER_MEMBERS=(app packages/taketray_ui widgetbook)` and `DART_MEMBERS=(tool)`, and `DST_MEMBER=app` with `DST_TESTS=(test/domain/rules/earlier_window_test.dart test/data/media/add_earlier_test.dart test/domain/rules/shooting_days_test.dart test/data/media/clip_time_test.dart test/features/shooting_mode/views/shooting_time_text_test.dart)`, the test files whose daylight-saving cases need Europe/London (step 9b). Every listed member must have a `pubspec.yaml`: a missing one fails the check, so a rename or deletion cannot pass silently (D-34).

1. `tool/check` › workspace guard: reads the `workspace:` list in the root `pubspec.yaml` (the `  - x` lines after `workspace:`, up to the first non-list line). Before that, a member listed in both `FLUTTER_MEMBERS` and `DART_MEMBERS` fails with `FAIL: member <x> is listed in both FLUTTER_MEMBERS and DART_MEMBERS in tool/check; it must be in exactly one`. Any entry that is in neither `FLUTTER_MEMBERS` nor `DART_MEMBERS` fails with `workspace member <x> is not covered by tool/check`, so a new member cannot silently escape the checks. Then fails with `FAIL: <kind> member <x> is listed in tool/check but <x>/pubspec.yaml is missing` for any listed member without a `pubspec.yaml`, with `FAIL: style directory <d> is listed in tool/check but does not exist` for a missing `STYLE_DIRS` entry, and with `FAIL: daylight-saving test <member>/<file> is listed in tool/check but does not exist` for a missing `DST_TESTS` file.
2. `tool/check` › SDK version: runs `tool/flutter --version`; fails with `Expected Flutter <x>` unless the output contains `Flutter 3.47.7` (`EXPECTED_FLUTTER` in the script).
3. `tool/check` › `tool/flutter pub get` at the root (resolves the whole workspace into the single `pubspec.lock`).
4. `tool/check` › `tool/check_generated` (see "Generated-file freshness" below): prints `stale: <path>` and fails when a committed generated file differs from what the generators write now (D-53).
5. `tool/check` › `python3 design/tools/build_tokens.py --check`: regenerates every token output in memory and compares it with disk (see "Token build"); prints `stale: <path>` for each difference and `orphan: <path>` for each leftover file in the generated-only folders, and fails; or fails on a contrast failure. Writes nothing (D-35).
6. `tool/check` › `tool/dart format --output=none --set-exit-if-changed` over `lib`, `test`, `patrol_test` of each Flutter member that has them, plus the whole of each Dart member (`tool`).
7. `tool/check` › analyze each member (rules from its own `analysis_options.yaml`): `tool/flutter analyze` in each Flutter member, `tool/dart analyze --fatal-infos` in each Dart member (plain `dart analyze` exits 0 on infos, so the flag keeps the zero-issue bar).
8. `tool/check` › `tool/dart run taketray_tool:check_styles <dir>` for each of `app/lib/features` and `packages/taketray_ui/lib/src/components` (`STYLE_DIRS`):
   1. `tool/bin/check_styles.dart › main`: lists `**/*.dart` under the directory (skips `*.g.dart`, `*.freezed.dart`; a missing directory prints `check_styles: directory <dir> not found` and exits 1).
   2. `tool/lib/src/style_rules.dart › findStyleViolations`: per file, blanks comments out of the whole text (`_blankComments`, offsets kept), runs each rule regex over the full text so multi-line calls match, maps each match start to its line, and skips it when `// style-check: allow <reason>` is on that line or on a comment-only line above. Covered forms: `Color*`/`Colors.`, `TextStyle(`, `EdgeInsets[Directional].*`, `SizedBox(width|height:)` and `SizedBox.square(dimension:)`, `BorderRadius[Directional].circular` and `Radius.circular`, and `Duration(` in any unit from microseconds to days.
   3. Back in `main`: prints `path:line rule snippet` per violation and exits 1, otherwise exits 0.
9. `tool/check` › tests in each Dart member (`tool/dart test`, which includes `tool/test/style_rules_test.dart`, `check_generated_test.dart`, `check_script_test.dart` and `check_coverage_test.dart`), then in each Flutter member (`tool/flutter test`, which without `CI` runs the `goldens/macos/` comparisons and not `goldens/ci/`, D-61; the member named in `COVERAGE_MEMBER`, `app`, runs `tool/flutter test --coverage`, which also writes `app/coverage/lcov.info`; the folder is git-ignored). A member without a `test/` folder fails with `<member> has no test/ folder`.
9a. `tool/check` › `write_coverage_imports`, just before the `app` test run (and nowhere else, so the format and analyze steps never see the file): writes `app/test/_coverage_imports_test.dart`, a test with one empty case that imports every non-generated `.dart` file under the `COVERAGE_PREFIXES` (relative to `app/`: `lib/domain/`, `lib/data/`, `lib/platform/`, `lib/features/*/view_models/`). A `*` in a prefix expands as a shell glob, so it is exactly one folder name. `*.g.dart` and `*.freezed.dart` are skipped (generated, D-53), and so is any file that starts with `part of` (a part cannot be imported). The script prints one count per prefix, for example `coverage imports: lib/domain/ 13 files, lib/data/ 13 files, lib/platform/ 0 files, lib/features/*/view_models/ 0 files`, so a glob that matches nothing is visible in the log. The task that creates the view models (Task 9) must see its count become non-zero there; a stuck 0 means the files are not being counted. (`lib/platform/` has counted since Task 3: `lib/platform/ 1 files`, `media_bridge.dart`.) The file is deleted right after the test run, by an `EXIT` trap if the run fails, and at the start of the next run if a killed run left it; it is git-ignored and never committed (D-65).
9b. `tool/check` › the step `daylight-saving tests (TZ=Europe/London)`: `(cd app && TZ=Europe/London tool/flutter test --run-skipped test/domain/rules/earlier_window_test.dart test/data/media/add_earlier_test.dart test/domain/rules/shooting_days_test.dart test/data/media/clip_time_test.dart test/features/shooting_mode/views/shooting_time_text_test.dart)` (the `DST_TESTS`; the last three, D-83, hold in any zone and run here so that a day count or a wall clock built on local `DateTime`s fails across London's 23-hour spring day, which CI's own zone, UTC, does not have; the second holds the same two days for "Add earlier videos", D-73, D-78: a chosen day and a range across each change, with the end of the window at the right midnight). Its London cases (2026-10-25, clocks go back, a 25-hour day; 2027-03-28, clocks go forward, a 23-hour day; a UTC `now` at 00:30 summer time, which is still the day before in UTC) skip themselves, with a reason, in any other zone, so in the run of step 9 they skip unless the machine is in London (CI runs in UTC). Here they run in London, and `--run-skipped` means they cannot skip: the case `the zone is Europe/London` fails if `TZ` did not take effect, and the others fail with wrong instants. The rest of each file runs a second time too. See D-69.
9c. `tool/check` › `tool/test_android`, the step `android unit tests`: the Kotlin media bridge's Robolectric tests (see "Android unit tests" below, D-67). Needs a JDK and the Android SDK.
10. `tool/check` › `tool/dart run taketray_tool:check_coverage app/coverage/lcov.info 80 lib/domain/ lib/data/ lib/platform/ 'lib/features/*/view_models/'`: at least 80 % of the executable lines under each prefix must have run in a test. Because of step 9a every file under a prefix is loaded, so a file no test uses shows up in lcov with all its lines at 0 and lowers the total.
    1. `tool/bin/check_coverage.dart › main`: needs the lcov file, a minimum from 0 to 100 (not `NaN`) and at least one prefix, otherwise prints `usage: ...` and exits 64. A prefix must end in `/` (so `lib/data` cannot match `lib/database_x/`); a `*` is allowed only as a whole segment (`lib/feat*/` and `lib/**/` are usage errors). A missing lcov file prints `check_coverage: <file> not found` and exits 1.
    2. `_parseLcov`: reads the lcov text, one record per source file (`SF:` path, one `DA:<line>,<times run>` per executable line, `end_of_record`). A file counts every `DA:` line as found and every `DA:` with a count above 0 as hit. Files ending in `.g.dart` or `.freezed.dart` are dropped (generated code, D-53). A file with no `DA:` lines (an enum, an interface) adds nothing.
    3. `_prefixPattern`: turns a prefix into the regular expression `^` plus the prefix, with each `*` segment as `[^/]+` (one non-empty folder name) and everything else escaped, so `lib/features/*/view_models/` matches `lib/features/a/view_models/x.dart` and `.../view_models/sub/y.dart` but not `lib/features/a/b/view_models/x.dart`.
    4. Back in `main`, per prefix: adds up found and hit over the files whose path matches it and prints `<prefix>  <percent>%  <hit>/<found> lines`, for example `lib/domain/  96.9%  62/64 lines`. A prefix that matches no file with lines prints `<prefix>  no files` and passes, because `lib/platform/` and the view-model folders do not exist yet (Tasks 3 and 9). If no prefix at all matches a file, each prints `check_coverage: no coverage data for <prefix>` and the run fails, so a typo or the wrong lcov file cannot pass. A percentage below the minimum prints `check_coverage: <prefix> is below <min>%`. Any failure exits 1 after every prefix has been printed.
11. `tool/check` › prints `ALL CHECKS PASSED`.

### Automatic checks on GitHub (`.github/workflows/check.yml`)

Runs on every `push` and `pull_request`, and by hand (`workflow_dispatch`); nothing is deployed and there are no secrets. See D-60.

1. Workflow header: `permissions: contents: read`; `concurrency` group `check-<workflow>-<ref>` with `cancel-in-progress` for every ref except `refs/heads/main`, so a newer run on the same branch or pull request cancels the older one, while main runs always finish; `env.CI: "true"` (turns the macOS goldens off and the Linux `goldens/ci/` comparisons on, D-61) and `env.FLUTTER_VERSION: "3.47.7"` (must equal `EXPECTED_FLUTTER` in `tool/check`).
2. Job `check` on `ubuntu-latest`, `timeout-minutes: 30`: `actions/checkout` (`persist-credentials: false`).
3. `actions/cache` on `~/development/flutter`, key `flutter-<os>-<version>`. On a miss, `git clone --depth 1 --branch 3.47.7` into that folder (the path `tool/flutter` and `tool/dart` use).
4. `Prepare Flutter`: `tool/flutter config --disable-analytics`, `tool/flutter precache --universal` (Dart SDK, the test engine `flutter_tester`), `tool/flutter --version`. `tool/check` builds no APK; the Android unit tests use the image's Android SDK (step 9).
5. `actions/cache` on `~/.pub-cache`, key `pub-<os>-<hash of pubspec.lock>`, falling back to the newest `pub-<os>-` entry.
6. `Check required programs`: fails with `missing on the runner: <name>` unless `git`, `python3`, `shasum`, `awk`, `find` and `sort` exist.
7. `actions/setup-java@v4`: Temurin JDK 17, for Gradle and the Android unit tests (D-67).
8. `actions/cache` on `~/.gradle/caches`, `~/.gradle/wrapper` and `~/.m2/repository/org/robolectric` (the Android framework jars Robolectric downloads), key `gradle-<os>-<hash of app/android/**/*.gradle.kts, gradle-wrapper.properties and robolectric.properties>`, falling back to the newest `gradle-<os>-` entry.
9. `Check the Android SDK`: fails unless `ANDROID_HOME` is `/usr/local/lib/android/sdk` with a `platforms/` folder (the ubuntu image provides the SDK there), and fails with `Flutter names NDK '<x>' but ANDROID_NDK_VERSION is '<y>'` unless `env.ANDROID_NDK_VERSION` (`28.2.13676358`) equals the `ndkVersion` in Flutter's `FlutterExtension.kt`; prints the platforms, build-tools, NDKs and `java -version`.
9a. `actions/cache` on `/usr/local/lib/android/sdk/ndk/<ANDROID_NDK_VERSION>`, key `ndk-<os>-<version>`. On a miss, `Install the Android NDK`: `sdkmanager --install "ndk;<version>"`, up to three attempts (a failed one deletes the partial folder), then fails unless the NDK's `source.properties` exists. Gradle needs this NDK even to configure the unit tests, because the `jni` plugin (used by `path_provider_android`) has a native build pinned to `flutter.ndkVersion`.
10. `tool/check` (the "Local checks" flow above), unchanged; with `CI` set it compares `goldens/ci/`, the images made on Linux by the flow below. Its `android unit tests` step writes `local.properties` and `gradlew` with `tool/flutter build apk --debug --config-only` first (they are git-ignored).
11. On failure, `actions/upload-artifact` stores every `failures/` folder under a `test/` folder as `golden-failures`.

Not on CI: Patrol and device tests, and `tool/check_release_permissions`.

### Updating CI goldens (`.github/workflows/update-goldens.yml`, `tool/pull_ci_goldens`)

The `goldens/ci/` images must be made on Linux, the machine that compares them (D-61). Two steps, one on GitHub and one on the Mac; nothing is committed or pushed by either.

1. `.github/workflows/update-goldens.yml` (`workflow_dispatch` only, `permissions: contents: read`; started from the Actions tab or `gh workflow run update-goldens.yml --ref <branch>`):
   1. `env.CI: "true"`, `env.FLUTTER_VERSION: "3.47.7"`; job `update` on `ubuntu-latest`, `timeout-minutes: 30`; checkout, the Flutter and pub caches and the Flutter install and precache exactly as in `check.yml`; `tool/flutter pub get`.
   2. In `packages/taketray_ui`: `../../tool/flutter test --update-goldens --tags golden`. With `CI` set `flutter_test_config.dart` enables only the CI goldens, so only `goldens/ci/` is rewritten. (`packages/taketray_ui` is the only member with CI goldens.)
   3. `find . -path '*/goldens/ci/*.png'` copies every image into `$RUNNER_TEMP/ci-goldens` with `cp --parents`, keeping its repository path, and prints how many; `actions/upload-artifact` stores that folder as `ci-goldens` (fails if it is empty).
2. `tool/pull_ci_goldens <run-id>` on the Mac:
   1. Usage error (exit 64) unless there is exactly one numeric argument. Fails with a clear message if `gh` is not installed or `gh auth status` fails.
   2. Fails and lists the files if `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` shows any change under a `goldens/ci/` folder.
   3. `gh run download <run-id> -n ci-goldens -D <temp dir>`; fails if the artifact has no `goldens/ci/*.png`.
   4. Deletes each tracked `goldens/ci/*.png` that the artifact does not have, then copies every artifact image to the same repository path (creating folders for new tests).
   5. Prints the number copied and `git status --short` for the `goldens/ci/` paths. The owner looks at the images, commits and pushes; the `check` run then compares them on Linux.

### Generated-file freshness (`tool/check_generated`)

Run by `tool/check` (step 4), or by hand: `tool/check_generated` (all members) or `tool/check_generated app`. Run `tool/flutter pub get` first. See D-53. Pigeon outputs are covered too (D-66).

1. `tool/check_generated` › start: `cd`s to the repo root and sets `root`. Without arguments it finds every `pubspec.yaml` up to three levels deep (outside `.dart_tool` and `build`) that has a `  build_runner:` or a `  pigeon:` line inside its `dev_dependencies:` section (`has_dev_dependency`); none found prints `no member with build_runner or pigeon in dev_dependencies found` and exits 1.
2. Per member, `hash_generated`: lists `*.g.dart` and `*.freezed.dart` under `lib/` and `test/`, `*.steps.dart` under `lib/` (make-migrations' step helper, D-120), `*.g.kt` under `android/app/src/` (Pigeon's Kotlin output), everything under `drift_schemas/` and everything under `test/drift/` (folders that do not exist are skipped), sorted, and runs `shasum` on each. The result is `path<TAB>sha1` lines, saved as "before".
3. `delete_generated_code`: deletes every `*.g.dart` and `*.freezed.dart` under `lib/` and `test/` (except under `test/drift/`), every `*.steps.dart` under `lib/`, everything under `test/drift/*/generated/` and every `*.g.kt` under `android/app/src/`. `drift_schemas/` is kept, because old schema dumps cannot be regenerated, and so are the hand-written tests in `test/drift/*/` (`schema_test.dart`, and `migration_test.dart`, which make-migrations writes only when it is missing). A generated file that no generator writes any more therefore stays gone.
4. If the member has `pigeon` in `dev_dependencies`: `(cd <member> && tool/dart run pigeon --input pigeons/<file>.dart)` for every `<member>/pigeons/*.dart`, first, so build_runner sees the Dart output; then `tool/dart format` on the `*.g.dart` files that exist at that moment (only Pigeon's, because step 3 deleted the rest), because Pigeon does not format its output and the format step of `tool/check` would otherwise fail on it. If it has `build_runner`: `tool/dart run build_runner build` regenerates the files. If `<member>/build.yaml` exists and contains `databases:`, `tool/dart run drift_dev make-migrations` runs as well. A failing generator fails the script.
5. `hash_generated` again, saved as "after". An `awk` join of the two lists collects every path whose hash changed, that is only in "before" (disappeared, which includes orphans removed in step 3) or only in "after" (appeared). It checks the working tree, not the commit: a fresh file that was never `git add`ed passes.
6. If any path was collected, prints `stale: <path>` for each and `generated files were out of date and have been regenerated; review and commit them` to stderr, exit 1; the regenerated files stay on disk. Otherwise prints `generated files fresh`, exit 0.

`app/lib/l10n/gen/` is not checked: it is git-ignored and rebuilt by `pub get` (D-27).

### Native bridge (`lib/platform/`, `pigeons/media_api.dart`)

The scanner, the verifier and the zone sync (`lib/data/media/`, the three sections after this one) call the bridge; the screens (Tasks 8 to 10) will too. The Kotlin side is the real MediaStore reader, registered by `MainActivity` (Task 4, "Kotlin side" below). See D-66. Paths are relative to `app/`.

**Making the contract** (by hand, when a new native question is needed):

1. `pigeons/media_api.dart`: edit the message classes or `MediaHostApi`; `@ConfigurePigeon` names the two outputs and the Kotlin package `app.taketray.media`.
2. `cd app && ../tool/dart run pigeon --input pigeons/media_api.dart` writes `lib/platform/media_api.g.dart` (Dart `MediaHostApi` class that sends on channels named `dev.flutter.pigeon.taketray.MediaHostApi.<method>`, plus the `*Msg` classes) and `android/app/src/main/kotlin/app/taketray/media/MediaApi.g.kt` (the Kotlin `MediaHostApi` interface, with `suspend fun` for `@async` methods, and `MediaHostApi.setUp(binaryMessenger, api)`).
3. `lib/platform/media_bridge.dart`: add the method to `MediaBridge` (domain types) and to `PigeonMediaBridge`; `test/support/fake_media_bridge.dart`: add it to `FakeMediaBridge`.
4. `tool/check_generated` fails (step 4 of "Local checks") when a committed `*.g.dart` or `*.g.kt` of the contract is not what Pigeon writes now.

**One call, for example the scan asking for new videos** (Dart side, today):

1. A caller (`MediaScanner`, through `mediaScannerProvider`) reads `ref.watch(mediaBridgeProvider)`, `platform/media_bridge.dart › mediaBridge`, kept alive, which builds `PigeonMediaBridge()`.
2. `media_bridge.dart › PigeonMediaBridge.videosAddedAfter(volumeName, afterGeneration, folders, limit)` calls the same method on its `MediaHostApi` (`media_api.g.dart`).
3. `media_api.g.dart › MediaHostApi.videosAddedAfter` sends `[volumeName, afterGeneration, folders, limit]` on the channel with `BasicMessageChannel` and the generated codec. **[bridge]**
4. Kotlin: `MediaHostApi.setUp` (in `MediaApi.g.kt`) decodes the arguments and launches `MediaHostApiImpl.videosAddedAfter` in a coroutine on `Dispatchers.Main`; the method moves to `Dispatchers.IO` for the query (see "Kotlin side" below), and the reply is sent on the main thread when it returns.
5. The answer returns the other way: a reply list holding the `VideoRowMsg` list, or an error list. `media_api.g.dart › _extractReplyValueOrThrow` throws a `PlatformException` for an error list, for a missing reply (code `channel-error`: nobody answered on the channel) and for a null where a value is required (code `null-error`). `PigeonMediaBridge` does not catch it.
6. `media_bridge.dart › _videos` maps each `VideoRowMsg` to a `MediaVideo` (same fields; a null `mimeType` becomes `video/*`), and other calls map their messages the same way: `_access` for `AccessReportMsg` (level enum), `volumes` to `MediaVolume`, `videoFolders` to `VideoFolder`, `pendingZoneChanges` to `ZoneEntry(at: DateTime.fromMillisecondsSinceEpoch(atMs, isUtc: true), zoneId)`; `zoneOffsetsMs` passes its lists through as they are.

**Kotlin side** (`android/app/src/main/kotlin/app/taketray/`; D-66, D-67, D-68):

1. `MainActivity.configureFlutterEngine`: `super` (plugins), then `MediaHostApi.setUp(messenger, MediaHostApiImpl(this))`, then the background bridge ("Background bridge" below), then `DebugHooks.register(engine)` (empty in `src/release`, `src/profile`; the debug one registers the test media channel). `cleanUpFlutterEngine` unregisters both bridges (`setUp(messenger, null)`) and calls their `onHostGone`.
2. Synchronous methods run on the main thread inside `guarded(method)`: `accessReport`, `openAppSettings`, `currentZoneId`, `pendingZoneChanges`, `clearZoneChangesUpTo`, `openVideoCamera`. The `suspend` ones that read MediaStore (`volumes`, `videosAddedAfter`, `videosByIds`, `allVideos`, `videosShotSince`, `videoFolders`, `thumbnail`, and since D-112 `videosInFolder`, `thumbnailOfUri`, `readableUris`, `persistedUris`, `releaseUris`), and `zoneOffsetsMs`, run `withContext(Dispatchers.IO) { guarded(method) { ... } }`. `pickVideos` is the exception: it starts the picker and waits for its result on the **main thread** (`PickedVideos.await`, `startActivityForResult`), and only its `describe` step moves to `Dispatchers.IO` (see "The photo picker" below). `guarded` turns any exception other than a `FlutterError` into `FlutterError("native-error", "<method> failed: <ExceptionClass>")` and logs the same at warning level under the tag `TakeTrayMedia`; the exception's message (it can hold a URI or path) never leaves Kotlin (D-10). The rule itself (`guardedWith`, `guardedOnIo`, `failureMessage`) is in `bridge/BridgeSupport.kt`, shared with the background bridge; each bridge passes its own `nativeError`, because each Pigeon contract has its own `FlutterError` class.
3. **Access** (`MediaAccessChecker`, D-68): `accessReport` reads `level(SDK_INT) { checkSelfPermission(it) == GRANTED }`; when it is full it clears `media_request_blocked` (in `taketray_native` preferences); then `canAskAgain(level, rationale, blocked)` (true for full, for limited, with a rationale, or when not blocked), where rationale is `activity.shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale(permissionsToRequest(SDK_INT).first())`. `requestAccess` (main thread): no activity → `FlutterError("no-activity")`; a request already waiting → `FlutterError("busy")`; else it reads the rationale (before) and, through `bridge/BridgeSupport.kt › PendingPermission(0x7A71).await`, calls `requestPermissions(permissionsToRequest(SDK_INT), 0x7A71)` inside `guarded` (a throw becomes `native-error` and frees the slot) and waits. `MainActivity.onRequestPermissionsResult` → `onPermissionResult(requestCode)` completes it (another code, or nothing waiting, is ignored). The level and the rationale (after) are read again, `blocked = blockedAfterRequest(level, before, after, wasBlocked)` (`!full && !after && (before || wasBlocked)`, the shared `bridge/BridgeSupport.kt › blockedAfterRequest` with full access as granted) is saved, and the report returns with `canAskAgain(level, after, blocked)`. `MainActivity.onDestroy` / `cleanUpFlutterEngine` → `onHostGone` fails a waiting request with `FlutterError("host-gone")` and drops the activity.
4. **Volumes** (`Volumes.kt`): `readVolumes(MediaStoreVolumes(context))` takes `MediaStore.getExternalVolumeNames`, sorted; for each, `version` (`MediaStore.getVersion`, null when it throws `IllegalArgumentException` or `IllegalStateException`) and `getGeneration`. A volume whose version or generation read throws anything else is left out (logged by volume name and exception class).
5. **Rows** (`VideoQueries`): every query is `resolver.query(MediaStore.Video.Media.getContentUri(volume), PROJECTION, args, null)` with a `Bundle`: `QUERY_ARG_SQL_SELECTION` / `_ARGS`, `QUERY_ARG_SQL_SORT_ORDER`, `QUERY_ARG_MATCH_PENDING`, and `QUERY_ARG_MATCH_TRASHED = MATCH_EXCLUDE`. A null cursor is `FlutterError("no-cursor")`, never an empty list. Each row goes through `rowFrom` (columns by name; null SIZE → 0, null DISPLAY_NAME / RELATIVE_PATH → "", the other nullable columns stay null, IS_PENDING 1 → true).
   - `videosAddedAfter`: no folders or `limit <= 0` → `[]` without a query. Else `GENERATION_ADDED > ? AND (RELATIVE_PATH = ? COLLATE NOCASE OR ...)`, each folder `trim('/') + "/"`, pending included, `GENERATION_ADDED ASC, _ID ASC`, `QUERY_ARG_LIMIT` (capped at `Int.MAX_VALUE`).
   - `videosShotSince`: no folders → `[]`. Else `(folders) AND (DATE_TAKEN >= ? OR DATE_ADDED >= ? OR DATE_MODIFIED >= ? OR DATE_ADDED IS NULL)` with `sinceMs` and twice `floorDiv(sinceMs, 1000)`, pending excluded, `_ID ASC`.
   - `allVideos`: no selection, pending excluded, `_ID ASC`.
   - `videosByIds`: ids de-duplicated and chunked by 500, `_ID IN (?,...)` per chunk, pending included; the chunks' rows are concatenated (order not part of the contract).
   - `videoFolders`: for each volume in `names()`, reads RELATIVE_PATH of every finished video (pending excluded) and counts per path in Kotlin; sorted by volume, then path.
   - An empty folder list in a selection is `0` (matches nothing), never invalid SQL.
6. **Thumbnails** (`Thumbnails.load`): `ContentUris.withAppendedId(Video.Media.getContentUri(volume), id)`, `resolver.loadThumbnail(uri, Size(w, h), null)` (each side capped at 4096; zero or less is `native-error`); `FileNotFoundException`/`IOException`, `SecurityException` and `OperationCanceledException` → null; the bitmap is scaled down until its longer side is at most `max(w, h)` and encoded as JPEG quality 85.
7. **Zones** (`ZoneChangeStore`, `TimeZoneChangedReceiver`): the manifest receiver (not exported) gets `ACTION_TIMEZONE_CHANGED`, even when the app is closed, and calls `record(System.currentTimeMillis(), EXTRA_TIMEZONE ?: TimeZone.getDefault().id)`. `record` appends only when the zone differs from the last entry and keeps the newest 500; the list is a JSON array under `zone_changes` in `taketray_native` preferences, written with `commit()`; corrupt JSON reads as empty. `pendingZoneChanges` returns it oldest first; `clearZoneChangesUpTo(atMs)` keeps entries after `atMs`. `currentZoneId` is `TimeZone.getDefault().id`. `zoneOffsetsMs(zoneIds, instantsMs)` (on `Dispatchers.IO`) calls `ZoneOffsets.offsetsMs`: `require` the same length (else `IllegalArgumentException`, so `native-error`), then for each index `ZoneId.of(id).rules` (looked up once per id per batch; a `DateTimeException` gives null) `.getOffset(Instant.ofEpochMilli(ms)).totalSeconds * 1000` (D-83).
8. **Camera and Settings**: `openVideoCamera` starts `MediaStore.INTENT_ACTION_VIDEO_CAMERA` and returns false on `ActivityNotFoundException`; `openAppSettings` starts `ACTION_APPLICATION_DETAILS_SETTINGS` for `package:<this app>`.

**In tests:** `test/support/fake_media_bridge.dart › FakeMediaBridge` implements `MediaBridge` from lists the test sets. `videosAddedAfter` keeps rows with a higher generation in a listed folder (each asked folder gets one trailing `/`; the stored path is compared as it is, ignoring the case of A to Z only, like `COLLATE NOCASE`), pending rows included, oldest generation first, at most `limit`; `videosShotSince` is the superset the contract describes and leaves pending rows out; `allVideos` leaves pending rows out; `videosByIds` returns the rows that exist. `failNext(method)` makes the next call throw a `PlatformException`; `gate(method)` pauses the next call until the returned `Completer` completes (the answer is read when the call starts); `calls` is the log of method names; `zoneOffsetsMs` answers from `zoneRules` (zone id to a function of the instant: `UTC`, `Asia/Kolkata`, and `Europe/London` by `londonOffsetMs`; a zone not there is null), throws `native-error` for lists of different lengths, and logs each request in `zoneOffsetRequests`. `test/platform/media_bridge_test.dart` tests `PigeonMediaBridge` against a stub `MediaHostApi` and, for the wire format and error replies, against the generated channels with a mock binary messenger.

### Folder pages and the photo picker (`lib/platform/media_bridge.dart`, `media/PickedVideos.kt`, D-112)

Six more calls on the media bridge (Pigeon contract `pigeons/media_api.dart`; regenerate with `cd app && ../tool/dart run pigeon --input pigeons/media_api.dart`, then `tool/check_generated`). Dart types: a folder page is `List<MediaVideo>`; a picked video is `PickedVideo` (`lib/domain/models/media.dart`). Never log an address, a name or a folder (D-10).

**One folder page, for example the video grid asking for the next 60** (Dart side, today):

1. A caller reads `mediaBridgeProvider` and calls `PigeonMediaBridge.videosInFolder(volumeName, relativePath, limit, offset)`; it sends the four values on the `videosInFolder` channel and maps each `VideoRowMsg` with `_videos` (the same mapping as every other video call).
2. Kotlin `MediaHostApiImpl.videosInFolder` (`Dispatchers.IO`): `limit <= 0` or a volume not in `volumeSource.names()` is `[]` without a query. Else `query(volume, PROJECTION, VideoQueries.inFolderArgs(relativePath, limit, offset))`: selection `(RELATIVE_PATH = ? COLLATE NOCASE) AND DURATION > 0` (the argument is `trim('/') + "/"`; a root path is `(RELATIVE_PATH IS NULL OR RELATIVE_PATH = '') AND DURATION > 0`), `QUERY_ARG_MATCH_PENDING` and `_TRASHED` both `MATCH_EXCLUDE`, sort `VideoQueries.SHOT_NEWEST_ORDER` (`COALESCE(NULLIF(DATE_TAKEN,0), NULLIF(DATE_ADDED,0)*1000, NULLIF(DATE_MODIFIED,0)*1000) DESC, _ID DESC`), `QUERY_ARG_LIMIT` and `QUERY_ARG_OFFSET` (both capped at `Int.MAX_VALUE`, a negative offset is 0).
3. An `IllegalArgumentException` from the query (MediaProvider's "Volume ... not found") is `[]` when the volume is no longer in `names()`, and the error otherwise. A null cursor is `FlutterError("no-cursor")`. A page of exactly `limit` rows means there may be more; a shorter page is the end.

**The photo picker, "Pick videos yourself"** (Manual mode calls it: the sheet, D-115):

1. `PigeonMediaBridge.pickVideos()` → Kotlin `MediaHostApiImpl.pickVideos` (main thread): no activity → `no-activity`; a picker already open (`PickedVideos.isWaiting`) → `FlutterError("busy")`. Else `PickedVideos.await { startActivityForResult(PickedVideos.intent(SDK_INT, R extension level), 0x7A73) }`.
2. `PickedVideos.intent(sdk, extensionLevel)`: API 33 and later, or API 30 to 32 with `SdkExtensions.getExtensionVersion(R) >= 2` → `ACTION_PICK_IMAGES`, type `video/*`, `EXTRA_PICK_IMAGES_MAX = MediaStore.getPickImagesMaxLimit()`. Otherwise `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT`, `CATEGORY_OPENABLE`, type `video/*`, `EXTRA_ALLOW_MULTIPLE`, `FLAG_GRANT_READ_URI_PERMISSION or FLAG_GRANT_PERSISTABLE_URI_PERMISSION`. A phone with no app for it fails with `no-picker` and the slot is freed.
3. The user answers; `MainActivity.onActivityResult` calls `media.onActivityResult(requestCode, resultCode, data)` → `PickedVideos.onResult`. Another request code, or nobody waiting (the process died while the picker was open), returns false and drops the result. Otherwise the waiting call resumes with `urisOf(data)`: every `clipData` item with an address, then `data` when it is new; `RESULT_CANCELED`, or nothing picked, is `[]`.
4. `MediaHostApiImpl.onHostGone` (activity destroyed) fails a waiting pick with `host-gone`, like a waiting permission request.
5. Off the main thread (`Dispatchers.IO`), `PickedVideos.describe(resolver, grants, uris)`: for each address, a query with no projection, read by name (`_display_name`, `_size`, `duration`, `datetaken`, `width`, `height`, `mime_type`; the picker's columns have the same names); a refusal (`SecurityException`), no row or no cursor still gives the row with size 0 and name `''`; any other failure fails the call. **The facts of every address are read first, and only then does `takePersistableUriPermission(uri, READ)` run for each** (`persisted = false` when it throws, and the others are still tried), so a failure part-way through the facts leaves no grant taken by this call: none leaks toward the per-app limit, and nothing is released, which could drop the grant of a clip picked earlier (minors batch, T2-M4; pinned by `PickedVideosTest`). Dart gets `List<PickedVideo>`.

**Saved addresses** (all `Dispatchers.IO`): `thumbnailOfUri(uri, w, h)` is `Thumbnails.loadFromUri`: anything that is not a `content:` address (any letter case of the scheme) is null without asking (the loader is not called; a test pins it with a recording loader); the size rules and the null cases (gone, refused, cancelled) are those of `thumbnail`. `readableUris(uris)` queries each address with the one column `_display_name`: a row keeps it; only `SecurityException` or a cursor with no row drops it.
