Everything else is a failed read and the whole call throws (so a caller never reads "failed" as "gone"): any other exception, and a null cursor (the provider is not installed, or its process died during the call), as `FlutterError("no-cursor")` (fix round 1: it was read as gone). `persistedUris()` lists the addresses with a saved read grant. `releaseUris(uris)` gives up exactly those grants (`releasePersistableUriPermission(uri, READ)`), counting failures (logged as a number) and going on.

**In tests:** `FakeMediaBridge.videosInFolder` filters and orders as above (an unknown volume is `[]`); `picked` is what `pickVideos` answers (each one with `persisted` also joins `persisted`, as the phone's saved grant would), `pickerBusy` makes it throw the `busy` `PlatformException`, `readable` is the set `readableUris` keeps (in the order asked), `persisted` is the saved grants, `released` logs every `releaseUris` argument, `uriThumbnails` answers `thumbnailOfUri`. Kotlin: `PickedVideosTest` (intent per SDK and extension level, results, facts, readable, persisted, release; the saved grants are behind the `UriGrants` seam, the queries go through `FakeMediaProvider`; `ResolverGrants`, the real implementation, has its own tests on Robolectric's resolver: the read flag taken and released, `saved()` telling read from write), `VideoQueriesTest`, `VideoQueriesSqlTest` (filter, order and paging on real SQLite through a strict builder), `MediaHostApiImplTest` (the gone volume, the busy picker, off the main thread).

### Picked clips: pictures, checks and the grant clean-up (`thumbnail_cache.dart`, `media_verifier.dart`, `picked_grants.dart`, Phase 7 Task 4, D-74, D-84)

A picked clip (`ClipSource.picker`, D-111) has a picker address and no MediaStore number. Paths are relative to `app/lib/`. No log line in any of these holds an address (D-10).

**Its picture** (`data/media/thumbnail_cache.dart`, D-74 as amended):
1. `ClipThumbnailKey.of(clip, sizePx)`: a picked clip with a non-empty `pickerUri` gets a key (address, `sizeBytes`, date taken in ms, the clamped pixel size); none for no address or `''`.
2. The file is `picked-<addressHash(uri)>_<sizeBytes>_<dateTakenMs>_<sizePx>.jpg`: FNV-1a 64 of the UTF-8 bytes, 16 hex digits.
3. A miss calls `bridge.thumbnailOfUri(uri, sizePx, sizePx)` with the 15-second limit. Everything else (disk first, temp and rename, shared loads, discard, prune) is the MediaStore path. Failures log `No thumbnail from a picked video: <code, timeout or type>`.
4. `ClipThumbnailImage` compares the source and the address too; its `toString` says `picked`.

**Covers and the backdrop** (`data/database/clip_rules.dart › hasPicture`): a clip that is not missing and either a MediaStore clip with a volume and a number above 0, or a picked clip with a non-empty address. A missing picked clip is not offered.

**Its check** (`MediaVerifier._verify`, inside the queued job, see "Checking clips still exist"):
1. After `clipsByIds`, the picked clips with an address are split off and checked **whatever the access level** (Manual mode is where they live): their addresses go to `bridge.readableUris` in batches of 500.
2. An address in the answer → found; left out → unreadable: **flagged `missing` and nothing more** (D-116). It stays in To tag, Skipped or its reel and is never deleted, because Android cannot tell an unplugged SD card or USB drive from a deleted video for a picker address; the address is kept, and a later verify that can read it un-flags the clip. (A MediaStore clip that is gone still leaves To tag and Skipped.) A picked clip that Android pruned at the 512-grant cap is the same case.
3. **Any throw from any batch** (a `PlatformException` or anything else) → every picked clip of this verify is `skipped` and nothing about them is written; it is logged as `Could not check <n> picked clips (<code or type>): skipped`.
4. Their found joins the MediaStore ones, and their unreadable ones go to `flagOnly`, in the one `applyVerification` write. The log line `Verified …` counts them as `<n> picked unreadable`.

**The grant clean-up** (`data/media/picked_grants.dart › PickedGrants.sweep`, `pickedGrantsProvider`, kept alive):
1. `MediaLifecycle` queues it once per app start, on the first frame, before the run's scan (see "App start and resume"), through `guarded` (`The picked grants clean-up failed: <type>`).
2. On `MediaWorkQueue`: `bridge.persistedUris()` (15-second limit) **first**. None → 0, **the database is not read** (a grant taken after this read is not in the list it releases from). Pinned by `sweep order and the spelling guard` in `picked_grants_test.dart` (three tests that fail when the two reads are swapped).
3. Every `clips.picker_uri` that is not null, whatever the clip's state (missing, skipped, in a reel, in To tag).
4. A saved address is held when a row has it as written or as `Uri.tryParse` normalises it (host case, `%` escape case); the rest are unused.
5. **Spelling guard (final review M-6):** if rows hold addresses, grants are saved, and not one saved grant is held, nothing is released and `None of <m> saved grants matches any of <k> addresses held by clips: released none` is logged (counts only); returns 0.
6. Unused → one `bridge.releaseUris(unused)` (15-second limit); none → no call. Logs `Released <n> of <m> saved grants (<k> addresses held by clips)`, returns `n`.
7. A failed or late `persistedUris`, or a failed database read, throws before anything is released; a failed release is thrown too. The lifecycle logs the type; nothing else waits for it.

Android keeps at most 512 saved grants per app and, past that, silently releases the oldest (no error: AOSP `maybePrunePersistedUriGrantsLocked`, D-112 as amended). A pruned clip shows as unreadable above. `BackupService.importJson` does not run on the media queue; no sweep can overlap it because the sweep runs only at start (Phase 10 must keep it so, `later.md`).

**The reel picker drops gone clips** (`features/reels/view_models/reel_clip_actions.dart › addToReels`, Phase 5 later item): the picker holds its clip ids while it is open, so before writing, the ids are read again (`clipsByIds`) and the ones that no longer exist are dropped (logged as a count). `clipCount` is the clips still there. When none is left nothing is written; `pickAndPerform` then shows no snack and does not set `LastTags` (as for a move of nothing). Both pickers (add on the reel page and the viewer, tag on To tag) go through it; move already ignored clips that had left the reel.

### Background bridge (`lib/platform/background_bridge.dart`, `pigeons/background_api.dart`, D-103)

Dart keeps a mirror of what the code that runs while TakeTray is closed needs (Tasks 4 and 5: the new-clips job, the reminders) in Kotlin's own storage, and gets back two things: a "Still shooting?" button tapped in the shade, and the screen a notification tap asks for. `BackgroundSync` writes the mirror and `ShadeActions` applies the shade's buttons (D-106, below); Task 7 listens for the taps. Paths are relative to `app/`; Kotlin files are in `android/app/src/main/kotlin/app/taketray/`.

**Making the contract:** as for the media bridge ("Native bridge" above), with `pigeons/background_api.dart` → `lib/platform/background_api.g.dart` and `background/BackgroundApi.g.kt` (package `app.taketray.background`); `cd app && ../tool/dart run pigeon --input pigeons/background_api.dart`. `tool/check_generated` regenerates every `pigeons/*.dart`, so it covers this one too. The contract has a host API (`BackgroundHostApi`, Dart calls Kotlin) and a Flutter API (`BackgroundFlutterApi.onLaunchAction`, Kotlin calls Dart: the one native-to-Dart path).

**Writing the mirror** (`writeState`):

1. The caller, `BackgroundSync` ("Keeping the copy in step" below), reads `ref.watch(backgroundBridgeProvider)` (`platform/background_bridge.dart › backgroundBridge`, kept alive), which builds `PigeonBackgroundBridge()` and calls its `dispose` when the provider goes.
2. `PigeonBackgroundBridge.writeState(BackgroundState)` builds a `BackgroundStateMsg`: `since` → `sinceMs` (UTC ms, null while off); `baselines` (a map by volume name) → a `VolumeBaselineMsg` list **sorted by volume name**; `waitingShots` → `waitingShotMs` **sorted ascending**; the folders and the reminder fields as they are; `copiesReminders` → a `CopiesReminderMsg` list **sorted by reel id** (`lastCopiedAt` → `lastCopiedAtMs`, UTC ms; Phase 8, D-124). **[bridge]**
3. Kotlin: `BackgroundHostApiImpl.writeState` runs on `Dispatchers.IO` inside `guardedOnIo`: `BackgroundState.from(msg)`, `BackgroundStateStore.writeFromApp(state)` (**stored first**; under the store's lock, when a shade Turn off for this very session waits, an "on" state is stored and armed **off** instead, no session and no baselines, so an "on" write already on its way cannot re-arm the job, D-106, final review M-1), then `arming.apply(context, stored)`. `MainActivity` passes `Arming.background(armOverdueStill = false)` (the app's own write arms no still alarm for a due time that has passed: the in-app dialog asks, D-105, final review M-2): the channels, the new-clips job's part (`NewClipsArming`, "New clips while TakeTray is closed" below) and the alarms (`ReminderArming`, "Reminders" below). An exception from either step reaches Dart as `native-error`, `"writeState failed: <ExceptionClass>"`; a failed arming leaves the new state stored.
4. **The store** (`background/BackgroundStateStore.kt`), in the `taketray_native` preferences (`ZoneChangeStore.PREFS_NAME`):
   - `background_state`: the state as one JSON object (`BackgroundState.toJson`; `sinceMs` is `null` while off). `state()` reads it with `BackgroundState.fromJson`; a missing key, a value of another type, broken JSON or a field missing or of the wrong type reads as `BackgroundState.OFF` (off, no baselines, folders or clips, no reminder), logged as `background state unreadable; treated as off` with nothing from the value.
   - `shade_action`: the waiting shade action as JSON (`kind` `turnOff`/`keepOn`, `sinceMs`, `atMs`, `generations`); an unreadable one or an unknown kind reads as none. `putShadeAction` replaces any earlier one. `clearShadeAction(atMs)` removes it **only if its `atMs` matches**, so a newer tap waits for Dart.
   - `posted_new_clips`: an `Int`, 0 when missing or of another type.
   - Every write is `commit()` (a receiver's process can end as soon as it returns, losing a queued `apply()`); every method holds one lock shared by all instances.

**The shade action** (`ShadeActionReceiver` writes it, "Reminders" below; `ShadeActions` applies it, below): `pendingShadeAction()` → `store.shadeAction()` → `ShadeAction(kind, since, at, generations)` in Dart (`since` and `at` UTC, `generations` a map by volume name). `clearShadeAction(DateTime at)` sends `at.millisecondsSinceEpoch`.

**The new-clips notification:** `clearNewClipsNotification()` (`MediaLifecycle` calls it after a scan that ran, once the new baselines are in the copy: "App start and resume") → `Notifier.cancelNewClips()` (cancels id 1, `Notifier.NEW_CLIPS`) and `store.setPostedNewClips(0)`.

**Keeping the copy in step** (`lib/data/background/background_sync.dart › BackgroundSync`, `backgroundSyncProvider`, kept alive; D-106):

1. `TakeTrayApp.initState` calls `start()` once (a second call does nothing). It listens to `PhoneStateRepository.watchShooting()`, `SettingsRepository.watch()`, `ClipsRepository.watchPendingShotTimes()` (the pending clips' `dateTakenUtc`, oldest first, emitted only when the list changes) and `EditSessionsRepository.watchSummaries()` (D-124: a copy added or removed, a session ended, a reel renamed or deleted). Each event asks for a write; the first events ask for the write at app start.
2. **The write loop:** one pass at a time. A request while a pass runs sets a flag, and exactly one more pass follows however many came (so only the newest state is written after a running one). `dispose()` (the provider goes) cancels the four subscriptions; a pass already running finishes.
3. **One pass** (`_writeOnce`), all inside one `try`:
   1. `ShadeActions.apply()` first ("Applying a shade action" below). A Turn off tapped in the shade while the app runs has already written the copy "off"; the database still says on until it is applied, and writing that over the phone's "off" would re-arm the job. A failure is logged as `Applying the shade action failed: <ErrorType>` and the pass goes on.
   2. `pendingShadeAction()` again, then read the state from the database **now** (not from the stream events): `loadShooting()`, `SettingsRepository.load()`, `ClipsRepository.pendingShotTimes()` → `BackgroundState(shootingOn, since and baselines only while on, watchedFolders, waitingShots, eveningEnabled, eveningMinutes, stillReminderEnabled: true (Phase 10 adds the switch), stillAnswered, copiesReminders)`; `copiesReminders` has one `CopiesReminder(reelId, reelName, copyBytes, lastCopiedAt)` per editing session that has copies (`EditSessionsRepository.watchSummaries().first`: `copyCount > 0` and a `lastCopiedAt`; a session without copies, in any editor, has none), by reel id, whatever Shooting mode says (D-124). While a Turn off for the current session is still waiting (its end failed or could not run), the state is made **off** (`shootingOn` false, no `since`, no baselines, not answered), so the job stays stopped and no "on" overwrites the shade's "off".
   3. Equal to the last state the phone took → nothing (no bridge call). Otherwise forget the last state, `writeState(state)`, and remember it. The first pass after `start()` has no last state, so it always writes (every app start re-arms the job and the alarms).
   4. Any error in step 2 or 3 (the action cannot be read, a read, the write) is logged as `Writing the background copy failed: <ErrorType>` and nothing is remembered as written, so the next request writes again.
4. The apply's own database changes (the session ended, clips added, the answer stored) emit, which asks for one more pass: the action is gone and the state equals the one just written, so it stops there.
5. **`flush()`** (after a scan, "App start and resume"): asks for a pass and completes when the loop is idle; it never throws. It reads the database itself, so a baseline the scan just moved is in the copy even before the streams deliver it. With nothing new it completes without a write; while writes fail, it completes after logging.

**Applying a shade action** (`lib/data/background/shade_actions.dart › ShadeActions.apply` → `MediaScanner.applyShadeAction`, one job on `MediaWorkQueue`; D-106). Called by `BackgroundSync` (before every write). **Every scan does the same first**, inside its own queued job ("Finding new clips"), so no trigger can scan past a waiting Turn off. No queue job waits for another.

1. `pendingShadeAction()`. None → done.
2. `loadShooting()`. Not `isSession(action.since)` (Shooting mode on, and its `since` equal **to the millisecond**: the phone keeps `sinceMs`, while a stored `since` keeps a real clock's microseconds) → logged as dropped, for another session; `clearShadeAction(action.at)`; done.
3. Keep on → `PhoneStateRepository.markStillAnswered(action.since)`: compare-and-set on the session; writes `stillAnswered: true` into the `shootingMode` value (kept by `advanceBaseline`, gone with `turnShootingOff`). Then `clearShadeAction(action.at)`. A failing mark or clear here, or of a stale action in step 2, is logged as `<step> failed: <ErrorType>; left for the next try` and goes no further: a scan still runs. Only a Turn off may block a scan.
4. Turn off → the capped end ("Ending a session from the shade" in "Finding new clips"). Ended → `clearShadeAction(action.at)`. Kept for another try (a volume failed) → the action stays.
5. `clearShadeAction` removes it only if its `atMs` is still that tap's, so a newer tap stays. A failure of the bridge in step 1 or 4 throws (the caller logs it by type) and leaves the action for the next try; a Turn off whose session ended by then is dropped in step 2 and cleared.

**Notification permission** (`POST_NOTIFICATIONS`, a runtime permission from Android 13):

1. `notificationAccess()` → `NotificationAccessMsg(granted, canAsk, sdkInt)`: `granted` is `NotificationManager.areNotificationsEnabled()`; `canAsk` is SDK ≥ 33 and not granted and the permission not held (when it is held, notifications were switched off in Settings and the dialog would not show) and (`shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale(POST_NOTIFICATIONS)` or not blocked). Below 33, `canAsk` is always false. A grant clears `notification_request_blocked` (in `taketray_native`).
2. `requestNotificationAccess()`: below 33 → the same report, no dialog. From 33, as `requestAccess` in "Native bridge": no activity → `no-activity`; one waiting → `busy`; rationale (before); `PendingPermission(0x7A72).await { requestPermissions([POST_NOTIFICATIONS], 0x7A72) }`; then `blocked = blockedAfterRequest(granted, before, after, wasBlocked)` is saved and the report returned (logged as `notification request answered: granted <bool>, canAsk <bool>`).
3. `MainActivity.onRequestPermissionsResult` hands the code to **both** bridges; each answers only its own (`0x7A71` media, `0x7A72` notifications). `onDestroy` / `cleanUpFlutterEngine` → `onHostGone` fails a waiting request with `host-gone`.

**Settings and the phone:** `openNotificationSettings()` starts `ACTION_APP_NOTIFICATION_SETTINGS` with `EXTRA_APP_PACKAGE`, else `ACTION_APPLICATION_DETAILS_SETTINGS` for `package:<this app>`; when neither starts, `native-error`. `openBatterySettings()` starts `ACTION_POWER_USAGE_SUMMARY`, else the app's details page, and returns false when neither starts (`ActivityNotFoundException` or `SecurityException` for each). Both need the activity (`no-activity`). `phoneMaker()` is `Build.MANUFACTURER.trim().lowercase(Locale.ROOT)`; it is never logged.

**Launch actions** (a notification tap opening a screen; `background/LaunchActions.kt`):

1. **Deep linking is off**: `flutter_deeplinking_enabled` = false on `MainActivity` in the manifest, and `MainActivity.getInitialRoute()` returns null, because FlutterActivity otherwise takes a `route` extra from any launching intent. `MainActivity` is exported for the launcher, so either would let any app open any route.
2. A notification (the new-clips one's body and Tag now; the evening reminder's body and Tag now; Still shooting?'s body, which opens Reels; the copies reminder's body and **Done editing**, which open that reel) uses `LaunchActions.intent(context, action, reelId)`: an explicit `MainActivity` intent with the extra `app.taketray.extra.OPEN` = `toTag`, `reels` or `doneEditing`, the identifier `taketray-open-<value>` (`taketray-open-doneEditing-<reelId>` for a reel, so each reel's intent differs; a `PendingIntent` ignores extras when it compares intents) and `NEW_TASK | CLEAR_TOP | SINGLE_TOP`. `doneEditing` alone carries the second extra `app.taketray.extra.REEL_ID` (a Long), the only payload there is; `intent()` refuses a reel id outside 1..`BackgroundState.MAX_REEL_ID` (1_073_241_823) with `IllegalArgumentException`.
3. `MainActivity.onCreate` notes `restored = savedInstanceState != null` before `super`. `configureFlutterEngine` builds `BackgroundHostApiImpl(this, Arming.background(), LaunchActionRelay.forActivity(this, send = BackgroundFlutterApi(messenger)::onLaunchAction))`, registers it, then `launches.onIntent(intent, restored)`. `onNewIntent` (a tap while the activity runs, `singleTop`) → `launches.onIntent(intent)`.
4. `LaunchActions.read(intent)` gives a `LaunchMsg(action, reelId)`: it reads the string and the reel id and **removes both extras** (so an activity re-created with the same intent object gets nothing, and a reel id sent without an action is not left behind); unreadable extras → nothing (logged by exception class). Then: `FLAG_ACTIVITY_LAUNCHED_FROM_HISTORY` (opened from Recents, which replays the first intent) → nothing; a value other than exactly `toTag` / `reels` / `doneEditing` → nothing. The reel id counts only for `doneEditing`, and only a Long from 1 to `MAX_REEL_ID` (anything else, or another type, is `null`: still a Done editing launch, and Dart opens Reels); on `toTag` and `reels` it is dropped.
5. `LaunchActionRelay.onIntent`: a `restored` activity's action is dropped (after the process died, Android restores the activity with its first intent, extra included). A fresh `toTag` action first runs the relay's `onToTag`, which for `MainActivity` (`LaunchActionRelay.forActivity`) is `Notifier.cancelEvening()`: the evening reminder's **Tag now** (an action button, which Android does not auto-cancel) and body tap, and the new-clips notification's taps, all remove it (D-105, 6a); a failure is logged `evening reminder not removed: <Class>` and the action still goes on. A `doneEditing` action with a reel runs `onDoneEditing` the same way (`Notifier.cancelCopies(reelId)`: the **Done editing** button, like **Tag now**, is not auto-cancelled); a failure is logged `copies reminder not removed: <Class>`. Until Dart has called `takeLaunchAction` once, the newest action is kept. After that each action is sent with `BackgroundFlutterApi.onLaunchAction` (on the main scope); a failed send (no Dart handler) keeps it for the next take. `cleanUpFlutterEngine` cancels the scope (`close`).
6. Dart: on the first listen to `launchActions` (a broadcast stream), `PigeonBackgroundBridge` sets up its `BackgroundFlutterApi` handler, then calls `takeLaunchAction()`. The start action (if any) is the first event; taps that arrive before the take answers are held and follow it; a failed take is tried again, 500 ms later, up to three times in all (Kotlin keeps every tap until one take reaches it, so one failure would strand them), and the last failure is an error event (the `PlatformException`, unchanged). The take happens once, however many times the stream is listened to. `dispose` removes the handler.

**Errors:** as the media bridge: every handler runs in `guarded` (`bridge/BridgeSupport.kt › guardedWith` with the background `nativeError`), so Dart gets a `PlatformException` with code `native-error` and `"<method> failed: <ExceptionClass>"` (logged at warning level under the tag `TakeTrayBackground`), or the bridge's own codes (`no-activity`, `busy`, `host-gone`). `PigeonBackgroundBridge` does not catch them.

**In tests:** `test/support/fake_background_bridge.dart › FakeBackgroundBridge` records every successful write in `writes` (the last in `state`), answers from settable fields (`shadeAction`, cleared only for its own `at`, compared in milliseconds as on the phone; `access`, `onRequestNotificationAccess`, `maker`, `batteryOpens`), gives `startAction` as the first event of `launchActions` and sends taps with `emit`, logs every call in `calls`, fails the next call of a method with `failNext`, and holds the next call of a method with `gate` (it takes effect when the gate opens). `VmHarness` puts one (`background`) into every container and `ProviderScope` it builds, so no widget or view-model test reaches the phone's channel. `test/data/background/` tests `BackgroundSync` and `ShadeActions` on a real in-memory database. `test/platform/background_bridge_test.dart` tests `PigeonBackgroundBridge` against a stub `BackgroundHostApi`, the generated channels with a mock messenger, and the Flutter API through `handlePlatformMessage`. Kotlin: `src/test/kotlin/app/taketray/background/` ("Android unit tests").

### New clips while TakeTray is closed (`background/NewClipsJobService.kt`, D-104)

While Shooting mode is on, a JobScheduler job counts the videos added since the baselines Dart stored (the clips the app has not scanned yet) and posts one "N new clips to tag" notification after 5 quiet minutes (D-110: each new clip restarts the wait, no maximum) with the normal sound once, and keeps it up to date silently. No Flutter engine runs; the database is never touched; the scan when the app opens stays the truth (spec §8.3, §11). Kotlin paths are in `android/app/src/main/kotlin/app/taketray/`.

**Arming** (`background/Arming.kt`):

1. `BackgroundHostApiImpl.writeState` stores the state, then calls `Arming.background()` = `Arming.of(CHANNELS, NewClipsArming(), ReminderArming(nowMs, zone))` (the channels and the alarms: "Reminders" below). `Arming.of` runs every part even when one throws, then rethrows the first failure. `Arming.apply(context, state)` is `apply(context, state, keepNewClips = false)`; only the shade's Turn off passes `true`.
2. `NewClipsArming.apply(context, state, keepNewClips)` → `arm(replace = false, keepNewClips)`. Shooting mode off, or no full video access (`NewClipsCounter.fullAccess`: `MediaAccessChecker.level(SDK_INT, checkSelfPermission) == FULL`) → `JobScheduler.cancel(0x7A72)`, and unless `keepNewClips`, `Notifier.cancelNewClips()` and posted count 0. Otherwise `jobInfo(context)`: job `0x7A72` on `NewClipsJobService`, `addTriggerContentUri(TriggerContentUri(Video.Media.getContentUri(VOLUME_EXTERNAL), FLAG_NOTIFY_FOR_DESCENDANTS))`, `setTriggerContentUpdateDelay(300_000)` (5 quiet minutes) and `setTriggerContentMaxDelay(7 days)` (D-110: JobScheduler has no "no max"; unset means Android's 2 minute default, which would run it mid-shoot), not persisted. Android also runs it at once at 40 distinct changed URIs; the run itself then holds the post back (the quiet rule below). If `getPendingJob(0x7A72)` equals it, nothing is done; else `schedule` (a failed schedule is logged as `new clips job not scheduled`).

**A run** (Android starts the job 5 quiet minutes after the last change to the video library, D-110; a phone in deep sleep may be later):

1. `NewClipsJobService.onStartJob(params)` hands the work to one background thread (`executor`, a single-thread executor shared by every run) and returns true. On that thread: `newJob(context)` (`NewClipsJob.forPhone`: the real store, `NewClipsCounter.forPhone`, `Notifier`, `ZoneChangeStore`, `NewClipsArming()`, `Thumbnails.bitmap` at 1024 px, the clock and `TimeZone.getDefault().id`) `.run()`, then `jobFinished(params, false)`.
2. `NewClipsJob.run` › `update`: `store.state()`.
   - Shooting mode off → `Notifier.cancelNewClips()`, posted 0.
   - Else `NewClipsCounter.count(state)`: nothing (`NewClipsCounter.NONE`, no query) without full access or with no watched folder. For each baseline whose volume's `MediaStoreVolumes.version` equals the baseline's version (an unmounted volume's is null): `resolver.query(Video.Media.getContentUri(volume), VideoQueries.NEW_CLIPS_PROJECTION, VideoQueries.newClipsArgs(baseline.generation, watchedFolders))` → selection `GENERATION_ADDED > ? AND (<folders>) AND DURATION > 0 AND SIZE > 0`, `MATCH_EXCLUDE` for pending and trashed rows, `GENERATION_ADDED DESC, _ID DESC`. No cursor → `IllegalStateException`. Each row counts; its shot instant is DATE_TAKEN, or DATE_ADDED × 1000 when DATE_TAKEN is null or 0. The newest is each volume's first row; between volumes, the later DATE_ADDED.
   - Count 0 → cancel, posted 0. Count equal to `store.postedNewClips()` → nothing (never re-posted). `Notifier.canPost("new_clips_alert")` false → posted 0, nothing loaded or posted. **The quiet rule (D-110):** first, `counter.recording(state)` (only now, when a post is possible): a pending row above a baseline in the watched folders touched within 3 hours (`VideoQueries.recordingArgs`, `MATCH_ONLY`, limit 1; `DATE_ADDED` or `DATE_MODIFIED` between 3 hours ago and 1 minute ahead of the clock) is a take in progress, so nothing is posted or updated and a settle job is asked for in `QUIET_MS` (the check throwing counts as a failed count). Then the newest counted clip's time is the latest DATE_ADDED / DATE_MODIFIED of the counted rows, ignoring any more than 1 minute ahead of the clock (`NewClips.lastTouchedMs`); when `now - that` is under 5 minutes (`NewClipsArming.QUIET_MS`; exactly 5 minutes posts; no time at all posts) nothing is posted or updated (a showing notification keeps its count), and the run asks for a settle job in `QUIET_MS - max(age, 0) + 5 s`, at most `QUIET_MS` (a clip from the future, after the clock was set back, waits the whole 5 minutes). Else the newest clip's thumbnail (`Thumbnails.bitmap(resolver, ContentUris.withAppendedId(...), 1024)`; null, or any exception or `OutOfMemoryError` logged by class, means no picture), `Notifier.showNewClips(count, picture)`, posted = count (0 if it did not post).
   - If `store.state()` now differs from the state `update` read (Dart wrote during the run: a scan in the app moved the baselines and cleared the notification), `update` runs once more on the new state.
   - An exception from `update` is logged as `new clips not counted: <Class>`; the notification and the posted count stay as they were, and a content run asks for a settle job in `QUIET_MS` (re-review M-12; with Shooting mode off the re-arm cancels it; the settle job's failure is retried by Android instead).
3. `ZoneChangeStore.record(now, zone)` (spec §8.3a; every run, even while off; logged by class if it fails).
4. `rearm()` → `NewClipsArming.rearm(context, store.state())` = `arm(replace = true)`: off or no access cancels as above; else `schedule` even when an identical job is listed (the running job is still listed until it finishes). Scheduling over the running job makes Android stop it: `onStopJob` → `rearm()` once more, returns false.
5. `onStopJob` (Android stopped the run) → `newJob(context).rearm()`, returns false (no retry: the content trigger is armed again).

In debug and profile builds only, each run ends with `Log.i(LOG_TAG, "quiet-rule: recording=<bool> fresh=<bool> posted=<bool>")` (`NewClipsJob.debugLog`, guarded by `BuildConfig.DEBUG` at the call sites, so nothing is read in release; three flags, no time and no number; `tool/check_release_permissions` forbids the marker in the release dex), also after a failed count (all false and none).

**The settle job** (`NewClipsSettleJobService`, id `0x7A73`, D-110; `NewClipsArming.settle(context, latencyMs)`): `JobInfo` with `setMinimumLatency(latencyMs)`, `setOverrideDeadline(latencyMs + 60 s)` (the delay alarm does not wake the phone; the deadline alarm does), `setPersisted(true)`; scheduling replaces a pending one. A run (`onStartJob` → the shared executor → `NewClipsJob.runSettle()`) is the content job's `update` under the same quiet rule, then: a wait still needed → `settle` again; nothing to wait for (a post, count 0, off, the same count) → `cancelSettle`; then the zone is recorded and `arming.apply(context, state)` (not `rearm`: an identical pending content job is left alone, a missing one is scheduled, Shooting mode off or no access cancels both jobs and the notification); `jobFinished(params, !countedOk)`; `onStopJob` returns true. `NewClipsArming.arm` cancels the settle job wherever it cancels the content job. The debug hook `runNewClipsJob` calls `run(ignoreQuiet = true)`.

**The notification** (`background/Notifier.kt`, copy in `android/app/src/main/res/values/strings.xml`):

1. `ensureChannels()` (called by every `show…` and by `Arming.CHANNELS`, so both channels are in Android's settings from the first `writeState`): `new_clips_alert` "New clips", `IMPORTANCE_DEFAULT` (D-110; Android cannot raise an existing channel, so the old `new_clips` LOW channel is deleted by `deleteNotificationChannel` on every call), and `reminders` "Reminders", `IMPORTANCE_DEFAULT`, with their descriptions.
2. `canPost(channel)`: `areNotificationsEnabled()` and the channel not at `IMPORTANCE_NONE` (blocked by the user).
3. `showNewClips(count, thumbnail)`: id 1 (`NEW_CLIPS`; `EVENING` 2 and `STILL_SHOOTING` 3 are the reminders'), small icon `ic_stat_taketray`, title `plurals/new_clips_title` ("3 new clips to tag", "1 new clip to tag"), text `plurals/new_clips_text`, `setOnlyAlertOnce(true)` (the first post makes the channel's sound; a count update of the showing notification is silent; a post after a dismiss alerts again), auto-cancel, `VISIBILITY_PRIVATE` with a public version (small icon, "TakeTray", the title as its text; no picture). With a thumbnail: `setLargeIcon(thumbnail)` and `BigPictureStyle().bigPicture(thumbnail).bigLargeIcon(null)`. Body → `PendingIntent.getActivity(101, LaunchActions.intent(TO_TAG))`; actions **Open camera** → `getActivity(103, Intent(INTENT_ACTION_VIDEO_CAMERA) + FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK)` and **Tag now** → `getActivity(102, LaunchActions.intent(TO_TAG))`; each `FLAG_IMMUTABLE | FLAG_UPDATE_CURRENT`. Returns false without posting when `canPost` is false.
4. `cancelNewClips()` → `NotificationManager.cancel(1)`. Callers set the posted count to 0 with it: the job (0 clips, off), `NewClipsArming` (off, no access) and `clearNewClipsNotification` (after a scan in the app).

**Manifest:** `<service android:name=".background.NewClipsJobService" android:permission="android.permission.BIND_JOB_SERVICE" android:exported="false"/>`.

**Logs** (tag `TakeTrayBackground`): exception class names only (D-10), never a count, volume, folder or path.

### Reminders (`background/ReminderScheduler.kt`, `ReminderReceiver.kt`, `ShadeActionReceiver.kt`, D-105)

Three reminders run from alarms while TakeTray is closed (the third, the copies reminder, has its own subsection below): the evening reminder (only clips shot today that still wait) and, 14 days into a Shooting mode session, "Still shooting?", whose **Turn off** and **Keep on** work from the shade (on One UI the buttons show once the notification is expanded: the S24 folded it away even when it was the app's only notification, and groups it under another TakeTray notification; the body tap opens Reels, where the dialog asks, which is the way that always works). No Flutter engine runs; the database is never touched. Kotlin paths are in `android/app/src/main/kotlin/app/taketray/`.

**Times** (`background/ReminderTimes.kt`, pure `java.time`):

1. `nextEvening(nowMs, minutes, zone)`: `minutes` is clamped to 0..1439 (`LAST_MINUTE`); from today's local date, the first `date.atTime(minutes)` in `zone` that is **strictly after** now, trying the next date until one is. A local time in a gap (clocks forward) is the gap's end (`rules.getTransition(local).instant`); in an overlap (clocks back), the earlier instant (`ZonedDateTime.ofLocal(local, zone, null)`), so it fires once.
2. `stillDueAt(sinceMs, zone)`: `since` + 14 × 24 h (`STILL_AFTER_DAYS`, as Dart's `stillShootingAfter`), then the first 10:00 local (`STILL_HOUR`) at or after it: that day's 10:00, or the next day's if it is earlier.
3. `startOfToday(nowMs, zone)`: `toLocalDate().atStartOfDay(zone)`.
4. `eveningToday(nowMs, minutes, zone)`: the same local time on today's date (gap and overlap as in 1; may be before or after now). `localDay(nowMs, zone)`: the local date of an instant as `toEpochDay()`; the handled mark names the day of the evening an alarm stood for.
5. `dueEvening(armedForMs, nowMs, minutes, zone)`: the evening an alarm armed for `armedForMs` is due for now. `armedForMs` exactly today's evening and that time passed → it; exactly yesterday's evening and today's evening time not come yet (a late alarm after midnight) → yesterday's; anything else (older, another time setting, null) → null. The receiver uses it to name and count a delivered alarm's evening; a re-arm catches one up only within `EVENING_CATCH_UP` (3 hours) of it.

**Arming the alarms** (`ReminderArming`, the third part of `Arming.background()`; `ReminderScheduler(context, zone)` with the zone of the moment):

1. `armEvening(state, now, firing = false)`: `eveningEnabled` false → `AlarmManager.cancel(evening alarm)`. Otherwise (minutes outside 0..1439 log `evening minutes out of range; clamped`, without the value): `due = dueEvening(store.eveningArmedFor(), now, eveningMinutes, zone)`; **catch-up** = `due` when not `firing`, `now - due <= EVENING_CATCH_UP` (3 hours, inclusive) and `store.eveningHandledDay() != localDay(due, zone)`. `armedFor` = the catch-up's evening, else `nextEvening(now, eveningMinutes, zone)`; `setAndAllowWhileIdle(RTC_WAKEUP, max(armedFor, now), evening alarm)`; `store.setEveningArmedFor(armedFor)` (written only when it changes). So an alarm armed for tonight that is late (or for last night, just after midnight) is not replaced by the next one within 3 hours of its evening; after that (a boot or an app open the next morning), and with nothing armed for that evening (a fresh install, the reminder just switched on, a new time), it never fires at once. `eveningEnabled` false also removes `evening_armed_for`. The evening alarm is `PendingIntent.getBroadcast(200, Intent(ReminderReceiver).setAction("app.taketray.REMINDER_EVENING"), IMMUTABLE | UPDATE_CURRENT)`.
2. `armStill(state, now)`: off, no `sinceMs`, `stillReminderEnabled` false or `stillAnswered` → cancel the still alarm **and** `Notifier.cancelStillShooting()` (id 3). Else, when `store.stillPostedSince() == sinceMs` (handled already) → cancel the alarm only (a posted notification stays). Else `setAndAllowWhileIdle(RTC_WAKEUP, max(stillDueAt(sinceMs, zone), now), still alarm)`: `getBroadcast(300, Intent(ReminderReceiver).setAction("app.taketray.REMINDER_STILL").putExtra("app.taketray.extra.SINCE_MS", sinceMs), IMMUTABLE | UPDATE_CURRENT)`. A due time in the past fires at once, except from the app's own `writeState` (`armOverdue = false`, final review M-2): that arms nothing for a due time that has passed (the in-app dialog asks; an alarm already armed stays), while the boot, update, clock and zone re-arms still fire it at once.
3. Both alarms are inexact (Android may deliver late, never early), fire in Doze, and need no exact-alarm permission (D-101). Arming again replaces each (same request code and action). `ReminderArming` arms Still shooting? even when the evening throws.
4. **The evening reminder leaves the shade** (D-105, 6a). Android removes a notification for a tap on its body (auto-cancel) but never for a tap on an action button, so **Tag now** would leave it. After `armEvening`, `ReminderArming` calls `dropStaleEvening(state, now)`: reminder off → `Notifier.cancelEvening()` (`cancel(2)`; the new-clips and Still shooting? notifications stay). Reminder on and `Notifier.isEveningShowing()` (`activeNotifications` has id 2; otherwise stop, no query) and no `waitingShotMs` at or after `startOfToday(now, zone)` and no unscanned clip at or after it (`Receivers.counter(context).count(state).shotMs`; a failure keeps the reminder and logs `evening reminder not checked: <Class>`) → `cancelEvening()`. So tagging the last of today's clips (the app's `writeState` arms) and the next day's first write remove it; a clip waiting from today keeps it. The alarm path (below) does not call this. The `toTag` launch action also removes it ("Background bridge" above).

**The evening alarm fires** (`ReminderReceiver.onReceive` → `inBackground("reminder")`: `goAsync()`, then `Receivers.executor`, one thread for every receiver; an exception is logged as `reminder failed: <Class>`) → `Reminders.forPhone(context).evening()`:

1. `store.state()`; `evening = dueEvening(store.eveningArmedFor(), now, eveningMinutes, zone) ?: now`, read **before** re-arming (the evening this alarm stood for: yesterday's when delivered after midnight). Then `scheduler.armEvening(state, now, firing = true)` **first**: always the next evening strictly after now, armed before anything can fail.
2. `eveningEnabled` false → `Notifier.cancelEvening()` (a reminder left in the shade goes) and stop.
3. `day = localDay(evening, zone)`. `store.eveningHandledDay()` equals `day` → stop (handled already: a re-arm at once raced the late alarm, the clock moved back, or a trip west).
3a. **The quiet check (D-110, `settleEvening`):** `NewClipsCounter.count(state)` and `counter.recording(state)` (asked always here). A take in progress → defer `QUIET_MS`; else `NewClipsCounter.quietWaitMs(lastTouchedMs, now)` non-null → defer that long; a count or check that throws → defer `QUIET_MS` (logged `evening clips not counted: <Class>`). To defer: `store.setEveningDeferredFor(evening)` and `scheduler.deferEvening(latency)` (`scheduleQuietJob`, id `0x7A74`), nothing handled, nothing posted. Quiet: `cancelEveningDeferral()` and `store.setEveningHandledDay(day)`, before posting: posted or not, that evening is done.
4. `since = startOfToday(evening, zone)` (the start of that evening's day); count = `waitingShotMs` at or after `since` (scanned clips waiting in To tag) + the `shotMs` of the count taken in 3a at or after `since` (clips not scanned yet; 0 while off or without full access).
5. Count 0 → nothing is posted (quiet, even if older clips wait), and `Notifier.cancelEvening()` removes yesterday's reminder if it is still in the shade (it said "today"). Else `Notifier.showEvening(count)` (same id 2, so it replaces yesterday's in place): `ensureChannels()`; `canPost("reminders")` false → false. Otherwise id 2 on **Reminders**, title `plurals/evening_title` ("You shot 3 clips today. Tag them?", "You shot 1 clip today. Tag it?"), text "Takes a few minutes, one tap per clip.", `CATEGORY_REMINDER`, auto-cancel; body → `getActivity(201, LaunchActions.intent(TO_TAG))`, **Tag now** → `getActivity(202, …TO_TAG)`.

**The deferred evening** (`EveningDeferJobService`, id `0x7A74`; `onStartJob` → `Receivers.executor` → `Reminders.eveningDeferred()`; a thrown failure → `jobFinished(params, true)`; `onStopJob` returns true): `store.eveningDeferredFor()` null or the reminder off → `cancelEveningDeferral()` (the key and the job) and stop. `eveningHandledDay() == day` → the same. `localDay(now, zone) != day` (the evening's day is over) → `setEveningHandledDay(day)`, cancel, no post. Else `settleEvening` as in 3a: defer again, or post once. `ReminderScheduler.armEvening` with the reminder off cancels the deferral; with it on, a stored deferral whose job is missing (Android lost it) is scheduled again for `QUIET_MS`, a pending job is left alone. The job is persisted, so a reboot keeps it.

**The still alarm fires** → `Reminders.still(sinceMs)` (an intent without the extra does nothing):

1. Not on, `state.sinceMs != sinceMs` (an older session's alarm), reminder off or answered → nothing.
2. `store.stillPostedSince() == sinceMs` → nothing. Else `store.setStillPostedSince(sinceMs)` (**before** posting: at most one per session, even if the post fails or notifications are off), then `Notifier.showStillShooting(sinceMs)`: id 3 on **Reminders**, "Still shooting?" / "Shooting mode has been on for 14 days. Turn it off?", `CATEGORY_REMINDER`, auto-cancel; body → `getActivity(301, LaunchActions.intent(REELS))` (the in-app dialog, Task 7); **Turn off** → `getBroadcast(302, ShadeActionReceiver.intent(context, "app.taketray.SHADE_TURN_OFF", sinceMs))`, **Keep on** → `getBroadcast(303, …"app.taketray.SHADE_KEEP_ON"…)`, each `IMMUTABLE | UPDATE_CURRENT`.

**A shade button** (`ShadeActionReceiver.onReceive` → `inBackground("shade action")`):

1. `store.state()`. No `sinceMs` extra, Shooting mode off, or `state.sinceMs != sinceMs` → only `cancelStillShooting()`. Nothing is recorded.
2. **Turn off:** `generations(Receivers.volumes(context), state)`: for each **mounted volume** (`Volumes.names()`, sorted), the volume's current generation (`MediaStore.getGeneration`) when it has no baseline yet (an SD card inserted during the session, or one whose read failed at switch-on: its session clips arrive in the capped end, D-108) or when its version (`MediaStore.getVersion`) still equals its baseline's; a rebuilt volume (version changed) and an unmounted one are left out, and so is one whose read throws (logged `volume generation not read: <Class>`). `store.putShadeAction(ShadeActionMsg(TURN_OFF, sinceMs, now, generations))`; `store.write(state.copy(shootingOn = false, sinceMs = null, baselines = []))` (off as Dart writes it, final review M-4); `Receivers.arming().apply(context, off, keepNewClips = true)`: the job is cancelled, the Still shooting? alarm and notification are cancelled, the evening alarm stays, and the new-clips notification **stays** (those clips arrive when the app opens; its write of the copy, off, then removes it).
3. **Keep on:** `store.putShadeAction(ShadeActionMsg(KEEP_ON, sinceMs, now, []))`; `store.write(state.copy(stillAnswered = true))`; `ReminderScheduler.cancelStill()`.
4. In both, `cancelStillShooting()` runs last, even after an error.
5. Dart applies the stored action at the next app start or resume, before its next write of the copy if the app is open, and first in any scan (`ShadeActions`, the scanner's gate, D-106; "Background bridge" above). After a Turn off, the app's write of the copy (off) cancels the new-clips notification, once the clips shot before the tap are in To tag.

**The store** (`taketray_native` preferences, `commit()`): `still_posted_since` (Long, the handled session), `evening_handled_day` (Long, `localDay` of the evening the receiver last handled, posted or quiet; the evening's day, not the delivery's) and `evening_armed_for` (Long, the evening instant the evening alarm stands for, kept through a catch-up; absent while the reminder is off); missing or of another type reads as null. The copies reminder adds `copies_shown` (above).

### The copies reminder (`ReminderScheduler.armCopies`, `Reminders.copies`, `Notifier.showCopies`, Phase 8, D-124)

Three days after the last copies of an editing session were made, one notification per reel says the copies are still on the phone and that tapping it opens the reel to ask Done editing (R6: for any session with copies, VN or CapCut/Edits; its on/off switch waits for Phase 10's Settings).

1. **The mirror.** `BackgroundState.copiesReminders` holds `CopiesReminder(reelId, reelName, copyBytes, lastCopiedAtMs)`. A Phase 6 state stored without the field loads as an empty list (`o.has("copiesReminders")`); a field of the wrong shape makes the whole state read as off, as any corrupt field does. `usableCopiesReminders()` is what everything below reads: a reel id from 1 to `MAX_REEL_ID` (anything else could land on Phase 6's notification ids 1 to 3 or overflow a request code), a `lastCopiedAtMs` above 0, one entry per reel (the newest batch), ordered by reel id.
2. **When.** `ReminderTimes.copiesDueAt(lastCopiedAtMs)` = `lastCopiedAtMs` + 3 × 24 h exactly (`COPIES_AFTER_DAYS`; elapsed time, not calendar days, and not moved to an hour of the day; saturating at `Long.MAX_VALUE`).
3. **Arming** (`ReminderArming`, after the evening and Still shooting? parts, each armed even when another throws; also with Shooting mode off). `armCopies(state, now)`: `store.pruneCopiesShown(<reels in the mirror>)` (a reel that left the mirror forgets its mark); of the usable reminders whose `store.copiesShown(reelId) != lastCopiedAtMs` (not shown for this batch), the **earliest** due time → one `setAndAllowWhileIdle(RTC_WAKEUP, max(due, now), copies alarm)`; none → `AlarmManager.cancel` and the PendingIntent cancelled (never created just to be cancelled). The alarm is `getBroadcast(400, Intent(ReminderReceiver).setAction("app.taketray.REMINDER_COPIES"), IMMUTABLE | UPDATE_CURRENT)`: inexact, in Doze, no exact-alarm permission, and it names no reel. `ReminderArming` then calls `Notifier.cancelCopiesExcept(<reels in the mirror>)`: a notification with an id of 1001 or above whose reel left the mirror (Done editing ended its session, the reel was deleted) is removed; ids 1 to 3 are never touched.
4. **The alarm fires** (`ReminderReceiver`, action `app.taketray.REMINDER_COPIES` → `Reminders.copies()`): for every usable reminder whose due time is at or before now and whose batch is not shown (earliest first, ties by reel id): `store.setCopiesShown(reelId, lastCopiedAtMs)` **before** posting (once per `(reelId, lastCopiedAtMs)`: a failed post, or notifications off, is not asked again for that batch, as Still shooting?), then `Notifier.showCopies(reminder)`; a failure of one post is logged `copies reminder not posted: <Class>` and the others go on. In a `finally`, `armCopies` arms the next. A delivery before the due time (the debug hook, a stale alarm) posts and marks nothing and re-arms for the due time; a reel that left the mirror posts nothing; a second delivery, a re-arm, a reboot or a write of the mirror never repeats a shown batch, and a new copy batch (a new `lastCopiedAtMs`) is reminded again 3 days after it.
5. **The notification** (`showCopies`): `ensureChannels()`, `canPost("reminders")` false → false. Reminders channel, id `1000 + reelId` (`COPIES_ID_BASE`; one per reel, posting again updates in place; reels 1, 2 and 3 are 1001 to 1003, beside Phase 6's 1 to 3), title `copies_title` "%1$s copies are using %2$s" (the reel's name now and `Formatter.formatShortFileSize(copyBytes)`, e.g. "Goa trip copies are using 1.2 GB"), text "Done editing? Tap to clean up.", `CATEGORY_REMINDER`, auto-cancel, `VISIBILITY_PRIVATE` with a public version "TakeTray" / "Copies for an edit are still on your phone" (no name, no size on the lock screen). The body → `getActivity(1_000_000 + 2 × reelId, LaunchActions.intent(DONE_EDITING, reelId))` and the one action **Done editing** → `getActivity(1_000_000 + 2 × reelId + 1, …)`, both `IMMUTABLE | UPDATE_CURRENT`; the largest reel id (`MAX_REEL_ID`) gives the codes `Int.MAX_VALUE - 1` and `Int.MAX_VALUE`. A reel id outside 1..`MAX_REEL_ID` throws `IllegalArgumentException`. The reel's name is the one piece of user text; no video name, file name or path is ever in it, and no log line holds a name or a size.
6. **The store.** `copies_shown` (JSON `{"<reelId>": <lastCopiedAtMs>}`, `commit()`); missing, of another type or unreadable reads as no marks, and the next write replaces it.
7. **Debug only** (`DebugBackgroundHooks`, `src/debug`): `showCopiesReminderNow(reelId)` posts that reel's reminder from the stored mirror through the real `Notifier`, at once and without marking it shown (`no-reminder`, `bad-argument`, `not-posted`). The release dex has none of it.

**Manifest:** `ReminderReceiver` and `ShadeActionReceiver` are `exported="false"` with no intent filter: only TakeTray's own explicit PendingIntents reach them.

**Logs** (tag `TakeTrayBackground`): exception class names and the clamp line only (D-10), never a count, time, zone, volume or folder.

### Re-arming (`background/BootReceiver.kt`, `media/TimeZoneChangedReceiver.kt`, D-105)

A content-trigger job and alarms do not survive a reboot, and the alarm times are local times that move with the clock and the zone. Each of these arms everything again for the stored state, with `Arming.apply(context, store.state())` (`keepNewClips` false):

1. `BootReceiver` (manifest, `exported="false"`, a filter for `BOOT_COMPLETED`, `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` and `TIME_SET`; any other action is ignored) → `BootReceiver.rearm(context)` on the main thread with `Receivers.arming()` = `Arming.background(Receivers.nowMs, Receivers.zone)`. An exception is logged as `re-arming failed: <Class>` and never crashes the receiver.
2. `TimeZoneChangedReceiver` (`TIMEZONE_CHANGED`): records the zone (`ZoneChangeStore`, as before), then `BootReceiver.rearm(context, Receivers.arming { zone })` with the zone **from the broadcast** (`EXTRA_TIMEZONE`, else the default; an id `ZoneId.of` refuses logs `zone id unreadable; re-armed in the default zone`), because the process default may not have caught up yet.
3. What gets armed: both channels; the job (Shooting mode on and full access) or no job and no new-clips notification; the evening alarm at once only when it was armed for an evening that is due (`dueEvening`: tonight's once its time passed, or last night's just after midnight), at most 3 hours (`EVENING_CATCH_UP`) after that evening, and that evening has not run (a reboot, an update or a clock correction at 21:05, or at 00:05 for a 23:30 alarm, before the late alarm came still gives it), else at the next evening **strictly after now** (a reboot at 21:05 after today's has run arms tomorrow's 21:00, so tonight's does not fire twice; a first write after 21:00 with nothing armed for tonight waits for tomorrow, and so does a boot at 08:00 the next morning after the phone died before 21:00); the still alarm unless that session's Still shooting? was handled (`still_posted_since`), so a reboot never posts it a second time; the copies alarm for the earliest batch not shown yet (a due time that has passed fires at once; a shown batch is never shown again; a zone or clock change does not move it, 3 days being elapsed time).
4. The app's own `writeState` (every start and change) re-arms the same way.

**In tests:** `Receivers` holds the receivers' seams (`executor`, `nowMs`, `zone`, `volumes`, `counter`); tests make the executor direct, inject the clock, the zone, `FakeVolumes` and a counter on `SqlVideoProvider`, and call `Receivers.reset()` after. `ReminderTestSupport` finds the alarm for an action in `ShadowAlarmManager` and fires it as Android would (its PendingIntent's broadcast, delivered to the manifest receiver on the main looper).

### Notification taps (`lib/features/notifications/view_models/launch_actions.dart`, `lib/app.dart`, D-107)

Kotlin never sends a route, only a `LaunchAction` and, for Done editing, a reel id (D-101, D-103, D-124), so no other app can make TakeTray open an arbitrary location. The stream carries `Launch(action, reelId)`; `reelId` is null except for `doneEditing` (the bridge drops one on any other action).

1. `routeFor(launch, reelExists)` (async) is the allow-list: `toTag` is `Routes.toTag`, `reels` is `Routes.reels`, `doneEditing` with a reel id of 1 or more that `ReelsRepository.getById` finds is `Routes.reelDoneEditing(id)` (`/reels/<id>?done=1`, the reel's page, which asks Done editing once when the reel still has a session and no copy job of it runs; see "Opening a reel", D-128). A reel that is gone, a null, zero or negative id, or a lookup that throws (logged `Looking up the reel of a tap failed: <ErrorType>`, the type only) opens Reels. A reel id on any other action is ignored, and no lookup is made for it. The new-clips notification (its body and **Tag now**) and the evening reminder send `toTag`; the "Still shooting?" notification's body sends `reels`. **Open camera**, **Turn off** and **Keep on** never reach Dart as a tap: the first opens the camera on the phone, the last two are shade actions (D-105, D-106).
2. `LaunchActionsListener` is a widget in `TakeTrayApp`'s `MaterialApp.router(builder: ...)`, under the router and above the navigator, so it lives as long as the app. `initState` listens once to `BackgroundBridge.launchActions`; `dispose` cancels it; a rebuild does not touch it. The stream is broadcast, so a tap that arrives while nobody listens is dropped; that is why the listener is here and not on a screen.
3. The first event is the tap the app was started with, taken once by the bridge (D-103), so it is handled once. Later events are taps while the app runs.
4. A tap goes to its screen with `GoRouter.go(route)` after a microtask, not at once (the start action can arrive while the first frame is being built), and taps are opened **one after the other in the order they came** (a chained future): finding out whether a reel exists takes a moment, and a later tap must not be overtaken by an earlier one. `go` leaves any page above the tabs (the tag player, the clip viewer, the help screen), so the user lands on the tab.
5. An error on the stream (the start action could not be read) is logged as `A launch action failed: <ErrorType>` and the subscription goes on; the next tap works.
6. On the **Reels** tab the dialog of "Still shooting?" then opens by itself (see below).

### Still shooting? (`lib/features/notifications/`, D-107)

`StillShootingPromptHost` is a widget on the Reels screen (a `SizedBox.shrink`); `StillShootingPrompt` (`stillShootingPromptProvider`, kept alive) decides.

1. **When it checks:** after the first frame; on every resume (`AppLifecycleListener.onResume`); when the Reels screen is in front again after a tab or a page above it went away (`didChangeDependencies`); and when the last open popup closes (`PopupRouteTracker` notifies the host).
   **It shows only when Reels is what the user is looking at.** All three hold (`_inFront`): (a) Reels' tickers are on (`TickerMode.valuesOf(context).enabled`: the shell turns a hidden tab's tickers off, and so does an opaque route above, such as the tag player, the clip viewer or the help screen); (b) Reels' own route is the current one of its navigator (`ModalRoute.isCurrent`: a reel's page pushed in the Reels tab); (c) no popup route is open (`PopupRouteTracker.isOpen`). A sheet (`showTtSheet`: New reel, the reel picker) or a dialog (`showTtDialog`: delete reel) goes to the root navigator and leaves (a) and (b) true, so only (c) sees it. `lib/router/popup_route_tracker.dart` keeps the set of open `PopupRoute`s; `appRouter` gives the root navigator and each tab's navigator its own `NavigatorObserver` from `observerForNavigator()` (didPush, didPop, didRemove and didReplace update the set). A menu (`MenuAnchor`) is an overlay, not a route: the menus that exist (To tag, a reel's page) are on screens that cover Reels. After its reads a check looks again; if Reels is blocked by then it calls `release()` and waits for the next check.
2. **`claim()`** gives the session to ask about, or null. At most one claim at a time, and at most one **per app session** (this process; a claim not used is given back with `release()`):
   1. the controller's state (`ShootingModeController`, waiting for its first value; a listener keeps the stream running meanwhile, because a provider nobody listens to is paused and its first value would never come) must pass `stillShootingDue(state, clock.now())`: on, not answered, 14 days or more;
   2. then `BackgroundBridge.pendingShadeAction()` is read. If it cannot be read, nothing is asked now (logged by type) and the next check tries again;
   3. then the database's state is read again (`PhoneStateRepository.loadShooting`) and must still be due. The shade action is read **first**: the scan applies a Keep on by storing it and then clearing the action, so an action that was just cleared has already changed the state read next;
   4. a waiting action whose session is this one (`ShootingState.isSession`, in milliseconds) means the user answered in the shade: nothing is asked. An action of another session is not an answer to this one.
3. **The dialog** (`showStillShootingDialog`, a `TtDialog`): a `videocam` glyph, "Still shooting?", "Shooting mode has been on for 14 days. While it's on, every new video goes to To tag.", and **Keep it on** and **Turn off**.
4. **Keep it on, Back and a tap outside:** `PhoneStateRepository.markStillAnswered(since)` (compare-and-set on the session). `BackgroundSync` sees the change and writes `stillAnswered` into the phone's copy, and the phone cancels its notification (D-105: arming with the question answered cancels it).
5. **Turn off:** `StillShootingPrompt.turnOff()`: `ShootingModeController.setOn(false)` (which never throws), then it reads `loadShooting()` back and returns whether Shooting mode is off. Only then the snack "Shooting mode off" on the root messenger (looked up before the dialog opened, so it outlives the screen); a switch-off that failed shows nothing. `BackgroundSync` writes "off", which cancels the notification and the job.
6. **Both orders are covered, so the user is asked once:** the dialog first (steps 4 and 5 cancel the notification); the shade first (step 2.4: a waiting action hides the dialog, and the scan, the copy's write and the resume apply it; once applied, the state is answered or off and nothing is due).
7. A failure of the answer's write is logged by type and nothing reaches the screen.

### Notification permission (`NotificationAccessController`, D-107)

1. State: `NotificationAccess(granted, canAsk, sdkInt)` from `BackgroundBridge.notificationAccess()`, read at the first use, on every resume (`MediaLifecycle`, step 2b) and after an Allow. Several calls at once: only the last to start writes the state. A first read that fails is an `AsyncError` until a refresh works; no method throws.
2. **Asked once, when Shooting mode is first turned on** (owner ruling): `ShootingModeController._turnOn` calls `askOnce()` right after `askIfDenied()` (the video question) and before storing Shooting mode. `askOnce()` reads the permission fresh and asks only if the phone has the dialog (`sdkInt >= 33`), notifications are not allowed, and the phone-state key `notificationsAsked` is not set. The key is stored **before** the question, so a question cut short is not asked again. Two calls at the same moment share one run. A refusal, a failure or no answer never stops Shooting mode.
3. After that only the **Allow** button in Settings asks (Phase 10's onboarding takes over the first ask, `docs/later.md`).
4. **Allow** (`allow()`): reads fresh; allowed → nothing to ask; `canAsk` → `requestNotificationAccess()` and shows the answer; otherwise `openNotificationSettings()`. It returns false when the dialog or the settings page cannot be opened (the Kotlin side throws `native-error` when no settings page opens); the screen then shows "Couldn't open settings on this phone." A read that fails goes to the settings page.
5. `notificationsAsked` lives in the `settings` table like `mediaAccessWasFull`: not exported, deleted by an import, unreadable counts as not asked (logged by key name only).

### Notifications help, with the interim Settings rows (`lib/features/settings/`, `lib/features/notifications/`, D-107)

**Settings** (`SettingsScreen`, the third tab; Phase 10 builds the rest around it): a large app bar "Settings"; the group **Help** with one row, "Notifications not arriving?" / "Steps for Samsung, Xiaomi, Realme and more" and a chevron, which opens the help screen (once however fast the taps, `OpenOnce`); the group **Permissions** with "Notifications": "Allowed" and a check, or "Not allowed" and a tonal **Allow** button (screen-reader label "Allow notifications"). The Permissions group is left out while the first read is running; if the first read failed it shows "Not allowed" with **Allow** (the permission is unknown, and **Allow** reads it again, then asks or opens the phone's notification settings).

**Notifications help** (`NotificationsHelpScreen`, `Routes.notificationsHelp` = `/settings/notifications-help`, a child of Settings on the root navigator: full screen above the nav pill, Back returns to Settings):

1. The page: a bar with Back (the first child of the body, not `Scaffold.appBar`, so the title wraps at large text instead of ending in dots), the intro, the success callout "Your clips are never lost", one `TtExpandCard` per brand and the footer "Menu names can be a little different on your phone." The steps and button names are the prototype's, verbatim. 12 dp under each step list, 8 dp between cards.
2. **Brands, in the prototype's order:** Samsung; Xiaomi, Redmi, POCO; Oppo, Realme; Vivo, iQOO; OnePlus. `brandOf(maker)` (`lib/domain/rules/phone_brand.dart`) maps the phone's maker (lower-cased by the bridge; compared whole, trimmed and case-insensitive): `samsung`; `xiaomi`, `redmi`, `poco`; `oppo`, `realme`; `vivo`, `iqoo`; `oneplus`. Anything else is no brand. The maker is read through `phoneBrandProvider`, never kept and never logged.
3. **The user's brand is open** and has the badge "Your phone". A phone with no brand (or a maker that cannot be read) opens no card and shows no badge. One card is open at a time; a tap on the open card closes it. A card the user opened before the maker arrived stays open; the maker then only adds the badge.
4. **The button:** Samsung and Vivo, "Open battery settings", call `BackgroundBridge.openBatterySettings()` (their background limits are in the battery settings); the others, "Open app info", call `MediaBridge.openAppSettings()` (Autostart and battery usage are on the app's page). A false answer or a thrown error (logged by type) shows "Couldn't open settings on this phone."

### The time where a clip was shot (`lib/data/media/clip_time.dart`, `lib/domain/rules/shooting_days.dart`, D-83)

Where times come from: a clip's instant is `fingerprint.dateTakenUtc` (D-69); its zone is `Clip.timeZoneId`, set at detection from the zone timeline (`zoneAt(timeline, shotAt) ?? currentZone`, "Finding new clips" step 5); the offset of that zone at that instant comes from the phone's own zone rules through the bridge (`zoneOffsetsMs`, "Native bridge", Kotlin side point 7). Spec §8.3a.

1. A view model (today `tagging/view_models/to_tag_times.dart › toTagTimes`, which awaits `toTagControllerProvider.future` and runs again for each new list) calls `clipTimeServiceProvider` (kept alive: one `ClipTimeService` on `mediaBridgeProvider`) `› localTimes(clips)`. No clips → `{}`, no bridge call.
2. `_phoneZone`: `bridge.currentZoneId()` with a 15 s timeout; a failure → warning (code or type) and `phone = null`.
3. `_fill` (the first batch): the keys `(clip.timeZoneId ?? phone, dateTakenUtc in ms)` (a clip with neither has none). A key in the cache is moved to the end (most recent) and copied into this call's `known` map; the rest go in **one** `bridge.zoneOffsetsMs(zoneIds, instantsMs)` (15 s timeout). Each answer goes into `known` and the cache (`_remember`: insert, then remove the oldest while there are more than 4,096). A throw, a timeout or an answer of the wrong length → warning (count and code); nothing is stored.
4. Unknown zones: the clips whose own `(zone, ms)` is in `known` as null. If there are any: an info line with the count of those the bridge answered in this call (`asked`; a cached unknown answer is not logged again), and, when the phone zone is known, `_fill` again with `(phone, ms)` for them (cache first; a second bridge call only for what is not cached).
5. `_timeOf` per clip: the first of the clip's zone, then the phone's, whose offset in `known` is not null → `ClipLocalTime(wallClock: DateTime.fromMillisecondsSinceEpoch(ms + offset, isUtc: true), zoneId: that zone, isPhoneZone: that zone == phone)`. Neither → Dart's local time: the fields of `dateTakenUtc.toLocal()` as `DateTime.utc(...)`, `zoneId: phone`, `isPhoneZone: true`.
6. Shooting days (reel detail, Task 8): `groupByShootingDay(items, clipOf, times)` puts each item under `DateTime.utc(y, m, d)` of its clip's `wallClock` (or of `dateTakenUtc.toLocal()` when the map has no entry) in a `SplayTreeMap` (sorted by date; each list keeps the order given), and numbers the days with `dayNumber(earliest date, date)`: `DateTime.utc` dates, `difference().inDays + 1`. `zoneCity(zoneId)` gives the viewer's "{city} time" (Task 10).

**Wall clocks are UTC-flagged** `DateTime`s whose fields are the local time: read the fields or format them as they are (`wallClockTimeText`, `ttDayText`); never `toLocal()`. **Logs** hold counts and codes only, never a zone id or a city (D-10).

### Finding new clips (`lib/data/media/media_scanner.dart`, D-72)

Triggered by "App start and resume" (below, D-75): `MediaLifecycle` on the first frame and on resume, `ToTagController` when To tag opens and on resume, the access controller after a grant, and the Shooting mode switch after it turns on. Every scan applies a waiting shade action first (the gate, step 3 below). Every media job (scan, end of a session, Add earlier videos, verify) runs on the one `MediaWorkQueue` (`media_work_queue.dart`, `mediaWorkQueueProvider`, keep-alive): one at a time, in order; a job that throws fails only its own caller.

**Calling `scan()`:**

1. `MediaScanner.scan`: if a scan is already queued and has not started, return its future (calls while a scan runs share one follow-up). Otherwise queue a new one with `MediaWorkQueue.run` and remember it.
2. When the queue starts it, the scanner forgets it as "queued" (later calls queue the next one) and runs `_gatedScan`.
3. **The shade gate** (D-106), inside the job: `BackgroundBridge.pendingShadeAction()`. It throws → logged as `Scan deferred: the shade action was not read (<ErrorType>)`, answer `ScanDeferred`, nothing read or written (the next trigger tries again). An action for another session → cleared, then step 4. A Keep on for the current session → `markStillAnswered`, cleared, then step 4. A Turn off for the current session → the capped end ("Ending a session from the shade" below) **in place of the scan**; its outcome is the answer. No action → step 4.
4. `_scanOnce`.

**One scan** (`_scanOnce`):

1. `PhoneStateRepository.loadShooting()`. Off → `ScanShootingOff`. Nothing else is read.
2. `_prepare` (shared with the capped end, below): `bridge.accessReport()`. Not `full` → `ScanNoAccess`; nothing written (the zone sync below does not run either). Full → `markAccessWasFull()` writes `mediaAccessWasFull`.
3. Still in `_prepare`: `ZoneSync.sync()` (see "Zone sync" below) runs first, inside this job, so the timeline read next already holds the zone changes the phone saw while the app was away (D-72, I-2). A failure is logged by the error's type only and the scan goes on with the timeline it has. Then reads once: `SettingsRepository.load().watchedFolders`, `ZoneRepository.all()` (the timeline), `bridge.currentZoneId()`, `clock.now()`, then `bridge.volumes()`. A `PlatformException` in step 2 or 3 is logged with its code (never its message) and rethrown.
4. `_stillSession`: `loadShooting()` again: off, or another `since` → `ScanShootingOff`, nothing more written. Then `_scanVolumes` (steps 5 and 6, shared with the capped end) reads `PhoneStateRepository.volumeVersions()` once.
5. For each volume (with caps, the capped end below, a volume not in them is skipped first and logged as `not in the tap`), `planVolume(stored baseline, volume)` (`domain/rules/baseline.dart`, D-70):
   - `SkipVolume` (not mounted): nothing. A card that is not listed at all is not visited either. Its baseline, version and clips stay.
   - `Resync` (version changed, or generation went back): `LibraryResync.resync` (`lib/data/media/library_resync.dart`): `bridge.allVideos(volume)` → `ClipsRepository.mediaStoreClipsOn(volume)` → `matchAfterRebuild(those clips, rows, volumeName: volume, now)` → `applyRebuildMatch(volume, match)` (writes `clips.mediaStoreId`, `missing`, `lastVerifiedAt`; lost clips leave To tag and Skipped unless they are in a reel, Ruling Y) → when lost clips are still there, detached, `PhoneStateRepository.openRematchWindow(volume, those clip ids, the volume's generation, now)` ("Detached clips are matched again" below; these last two steps run in one transaction, `ClipsRepository.inTransaction`, so a window that cannot be written takes the detach back and the next scan does both) → `recordVolumeVersion`. Then `advanceBaseline(expected: the stored baseline, next: (version, generation), rebuilt: true)`. No clip is added. If the last write is refused (Shooting mode went off and on meanwhile), the remap stays and nothing else is written.
   - Otherwise, first `LibraryResync.catchUp(volume, recorded versions)`: same version → nothing; none recorded → `recordVolumeVersion`; another version → `resync` as above (the volume is reported in `resynced`). Then:
     - `StartFromNow` (no baseline): the clips shot since the session started ("A volume with no baseline" below, D-108). Nothing shot before the session is new.
     - `ScanAfter(g)`: first, except in the capped end, the re-match of the clips a rebuild detached ("Detached clips are matched again" below, D-108), then the paging below (with the volume's cap, if any).
   - A `PlatformException`, or any other error, from any of these puts the volume in `failedVolumes` (logged by volume name and the code or the error's type); its baseline is not written; the next volume runs.
6. Returns `ScanDone(added, resynced, failedVolumes)`.

**`ScanAfter(g)`, paging one volume** (`_scanAfter`):

1. `captured` = the volume's generation from step 3, or the cap when one is given and lower (the capped end: the generation at the shade's tap). `cursor` = `g`, `limit` = 500.
2. `bridge.videosAddedAfter(volume, cursor, folders, limit)`. Rows with `generationAdded > captured` are dropped, and the page that held one is the last. The others are kept in a map by MediaStore id (a row that comes back on the next page counts once; the later copy wins).
3. Fewer rows than `limit`: the last page.
4. A full page: `next = nextPageCursor(cursor, page)`. Not null: `cursor = next`, `limit = 500`, back to 2. Null (every row in one generation, or no progress): `limit` doubles (1000, 2000, …, 16000) and the same cursor is asked again. Still stalled at 16000: stop, `truncated` = true.
5. The usable rows (`isUsable`: finished, with a length and a size above 0) → `_commit` (shared with a new volume's first scan below) → `ClipsRepository.addCheckedClips([newClipFrom(v, now, zoneId: zoneAt(timeline, shotAt(v)) ?? currentZone)], sameVideo: stored clip vs its row)`: a stored clip holding a row's number that is the same video is kept; one that is not is detached (and leaves To tag and Skipped unless it is in a reel, Ruling Y) and the row saved as a new clip; new clips get a `clips` row and an `inbox` row `pending`. All one transaction.
6. Only after step 5 succeeded: `advanceBaseline(from: g, captured, seen: every kept row, now, truncated)` (`domain/rules/baseline.dart`), which holds just below the oldest row that `holdsBaseline` (pending, or finished without a length or with a size of 0 or unknown, and added at most three hours ago; a row that is not usable yet is read again by every scan in that time, and the cursor still pages past it). If that differs from `g`, `PhoneStateRepository.advanceBaseline(volume, expectedSession, expected: stored baseline, next: (version, generation))`, compare-and-set (a stale scan writes nothing).
7. Logs the volume name and counts (rows, pages, usable, new, held, detached, whether the baseline moved); a truncated scan logs a warning.

**Detached clips are matched again** (`MediaScanner._rematchDetached` → `LibraryResync.reattach`, D-108, I-3). For a `ScanAfter` volume, before its paging, so a video that is back ahead of the baseline is found to be a clip already and is not added a second time. Only inside a **re-match window**: `LibraryResync.resync` opens one when a rebuild leaves clips detached (above), stored by `PhoneStateRepository` under the `settings` key `mediaRematchWindows` (one JSON object by volume name: `openedAtMs`, `clipIds`, `generation`; not exported, an import deletes it, Shooting mode going off keeps it). A second rebuild while one is open adds its clips and starts the 24 hours again. A clip a verify detached never opens one, so it costs nothing.

1. `PhoneStateRepository.rematchWindow(volume)`. None, or an unreadable value (logged once as `Setting mediaRematchWindows is corrupt, reading it as none and removing it`, the key only; the value is deleted) → nothing is read.
2. `RematchWindow.isOpenAt(now)` (`domain/rules/rematch_window.dart`): open from `openedAt` for `rematchWindowLength` (24 hours, the end excluded). Ended, or a `now` more than `rematchClockTolerance` (5 minutes) before `openedAt` (the clock went far back) → `closeRematchWindow(volume)`, nothing is read. A clock corrected back by up to 5 minutes leaves it open.
3. `ClipsRepository.detachedClipsOn(volume)`, narrowed to the window's clip ids. None left (re-attached, or removed by the user) → `closeRematchWindow`, nothing is read.
4. The volume's generation equals the window's `generation` (the resync's, then each re-match's) → nothing is read: the same videos are on it.
5. `bridge.allVideos(volume)` (at most once per scan and volume), then `matchAfterRebuild(those clips, rows, volumeName, now)`: the rebuild's own rule, a unique fingerprint (the display name only chooses among several; a video wanted by two clips goes to neither).
6. `ClipsRepository.reattachDetached(volume, match.remap)`, one transaction (the table in "Tagging data changes"): a video nobody holds goes to the clip; a video held by a duplicate of the same video (it came in as a new clip, in To tag, in Skipped or in a reel) is taken from the duplicate, which is deleted after its reel links are merged into the old clip (the earliest `addedAt` per reel); a video held by a clip that is another video is left alone.
7. Every clip of the window back → `closeRematchWindow`; otherwise `recordRematch(volume, generation)`, so the next scan reads again only when the generation has moved.
8. Logged by volume name and counts. A failure of step 5 or 6 is logged by the error's type, before step 7, so the next scan tries again even at the same generation; the scan goes on: the volume is not in `failedVolumes` and its baseline moves.

The capped end never re-matches (it ends the session; a repair that reads the whole volume waits). A `Resync` volume (its own `allVideos`; the window it opens starts at that generation, so a `catchUp` resync is not followed by a second read in the same scan), a volume with no baseline and a volume that is not mounted are not re-matched.

**A volume with no baseline** (`MediaScanner._startNewVolume`, D-108, M-8): Shooting mode was switched on while the volume could not be read, an import left no baseline, or an SD card was inserted later.

1. `bridge.videosShotSince(volume, folders, session.since in ms)`, once. A `PlatformException` fails the volume (in `failedVolumes`, no baseline written, the next scan tries again).
2. `shotSinceSession(rows, since, captured, now)` (`domain/rules/baseline.dart`) keeps the rows with `shotAt >= since` (exactly at the start counts; the phone's query is a superset, so a clip shot before the session and copied onto the card now is dropped here) and `generationAdded <= captured`. `captured` is the volume's generation, or the cap when the capped end names this volume (it never does without a baseline unless the copy is stale; then no row above the cap is kept and the baseline goes no higher). A volume the capped end does not name is left alone, as before ("not in the tap").
3. `_commit(hold: false)`, the same end as `ScanAfter` step 5 to 7 except that the baseline never holds: the usable rows are saved with `addCheckedClips` (a clip that is already stored is kept where it is and not added again; one that holds the number of another video is detached); then the baseline moves to `captured` (`advanceBaseline(from: 0, captured, seen: none, now)`). A row that is not finished (`holdsBaseline`: no length or size yet, added within three hours; a pending row is not in the phone's answer at all) is not added, and the next scan does not see it: **the gap** (D-108). Holding below it would make the next scan a plain `ScanAfter` with no date filter, and every video shot before the session above it on the card (a card indexes in no order) would come in as new. "Add earlier videos" recovers such a clip. Written with `expected: null`, version = the volume's. A failure before this leaves no baseline.
4. Returns how many clips are new (`ScanDone.added`).

**Ending a session from the shade** (`MediaScanner._endCapped`, D-106). The gate above and `applyShadeAction` run it for a Turn off for the current session, with the action's `generations` (the generation at the tap of every mounted volume, with a baseline or without one; a volume whose version no longer matched its baseline, that was not mounted, or whose read failed is not in it). A volume without a baseline (an SD card inserted mid-session, D-108) is a `StartFromNow` here: its clips shot since the session started, up to its cap, are added (D-105, item 5). It always runs inside one of those jobs on `MediaWorkQueue` and never queues another:
